## assistant

I recommended 2.5 only because the bands were measured on it, not because it grades better. Here is the honest picture.

**What the difference actually is.** The judge is the model that reads each rephrased line against the golden standard and decides whether the meaning was kept. ADR 0017 and 0018 set the "full coverage" bands by running the golden standard itself through the judge on 2.5-flash and measuring where it landed. Those bands are a ruler calibrated to one model's strictness. Swap the model and the ruler no longer matches the marks.

**Which model is the better judge.** I cannot tell you from memory that 3.8-flash is a more accurate judge than 2.5-flash. Nothing in the vault has measured it, and I will not assert it. What I can say from the one disagreement recorded in the log is that 3.8 was stricter, and on that example it was arguably right:

- Celia said "even antibiotics that are strong still struggle to keep bacteria out."
- The standard said "even the strong ones fail."
- 2.5 accepted it. 3.8 marked "fail" as dropped. "Struggle" and "fail" are different claims, and on the LSAT that gap is exactly what the drill exists to catch.

So the early sign is that 3.8 reads more carefully, which is what you want for a drill built on precision. The cost of leaving it on 3.8 uncalibrated is that scores fall without the bands moving, so a student who did fine looks worse than the bands say. Coloured lines went 6 to 0 on the same recording, which means the colour thresholds also need resetting.

**My recommendation for the highest-quality drill.** Stay on 3.8-flash and recalibrate, in two steps:

1. Rerun the golden-standard census on 3.8 and reset the coverage bands and colour thresholds in a new ADR that supersedes 0017 and 0018.
2. Before trusting either model, grade the same handful of recordings on both and read every line where they disagree. Whichever model is right more often on the disagreements is the judge. This is the only real evidence, and there is none yet.

Step 2 is what I would do first, since it answers your question with data instead of my read of one line. It needs no code change beyond a model flag on the admin call. Say go and I will run it on Celia's and Charles's recordings plus the golden standard, and bring back the disagreement list.

No `calibrat*` script in `scripts/`; `classify_details.py` and `student_shaped.py` live outside this repo (`system/scripts/activity-centre/`).

## 3. Report Card UI — what the student sees today

Files: `src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionReportCard.tsx` (865 lines, the whole card), `SectionReport.tsx` (120, the pending/poll wrapper), `ActivityResults.tsx:211`, `src/pages/ActivityLab.tsx:1965`. Note it is under `components/`, **not** under `recall/`.

Present:
- Section band headline + mean coverage + Held/Understood pair — L329-400.
- Clock vs goal, with the "goal only counts if all coloured + Read" qualifier — L411-442.
- "Worth working on" habit list (reciting/listing/uncoloured/dead air/read-aloud counts) — L293-305, rendered L453-466.
- One-line "what to do next" advice from `recall/nextStep.ts` — L311, rendered L360-364. Section-level only.
- Collapsible per-stimulus rows, "Stimulus N", band or "Said back word for word", `carried of total` — L495-518.
- Per-stimulus missed details list ("What slipped") — L542-555 (flat `s.missed`, no line attribution).
- Your Read verdict, three canned sentences — L558-571. **`s.readReason` is fetched but never rendered** (only passed into a dispute, L827).
- Reciting / listing / uncoloured / colour-not-specific coaching sentences — L573-605.
- Dead-air sentence — L607-612.
- **Line by line block** — L625-691: authored line text, verdict chip, per-line "Hear it" clip button (L650-660), her spoken text for that line, judge `line.note`, `Dropped: …`. Then whole-recall verdict+note L675-684, then `Biggest misses` L685-689.
- Per-stimulus transcript ("What you said") + "Hear it back" (recall window, falls back to whole window) + "Hear it done right" (model-run audio) — L703-746.
- Golden standard text: `Walkthrough` component L211-236 renders `model_runs.walkthrough_md` (quote lines = the read, paragraphs = the recall), rendered at L771.
- Dispute control "This is not what I said" — L768, component L808-865, `recall/disputes.ts`.
- Marking-suspect / unmarked / missing-rows disclosures — L340-345, L475-491.

Absent / gaps for a fully interactive per-question report:
- The **stimulus text itself is never shown** — only the individual authored line texts inside the Line-by-line block; there is no side-by-side "source line vs your line" for a stimulus whose `lines` array is missing (older grades), and no full-passage view anywhere.
- **The Read key text** ("the read she should have arrived at") is not shown and is not even in the row — only `the_read_type` is stored and it is not rendered either.
- `line.carried[]` is never rendered (only `line.dropped`), so there's no "what you got" per line.
- The **golden standard is per stimulus only**, never aligned per line, and the whole Walkthrough block is gated behind `s.spoken || clip || model` (L703) and rendered at the bottom of the transcript block — it is not pairable line-to-line with her own lines.
- `decoysCarried`, `unjudged`, `read_aloud_words`/`chunks_read_aloud` per stimulus, `reciting_run`/`reciting_bigram`, `percent` per stimulus, `the_read_type` — all stored, none rendered per row.
- **No improvement advice per stimulus or per line** — `nextStep()` is section-level, one sentence; per-line advice is limited to whatever the judge put in `line.note`.
- No whole-section transcript view (`grade.transcript_text` unused in this file).
- No playback of the full recording, no scrubber, no play-all, no highlight-follows-audio; clip is fire-and-forget with a `timeupdate` stop.
- No per-line model-run audio (model audio is one clip for the whole stimulus, `clip.play(key-model, model.audio)` with no start/end, L731).

/**
 * Run the judge over Joshua's own recordings and see whether it agrees.
 *
 * The 71 model runs in `corpus/loophole-online/rrc-sections/coverage-census.json`
 * were scored by hand and by agent: a correct run sits at a median of 95%
 * Coverage with a 10th percentile of 86%, and that 86 is where the top band
 * starts. This harness points the real judge at the same runs and compares.
 *
 * It is the only check that can fail in the way that matters. The unit tests
 * prove the parsing, the scoring and the banding; they cannot prove that a
 * model, given this prompt, judges the way we decided it should. If the judge
 * scores these runs at 70%, every student would be told she failed at something
 * she did correctly.
 *
 *   npx vite-node scripts/grade-census.ts -- --shaped <dir> --limit 5
 *
 * Reports token usage per call, so the cost of a full run is measured rather
 * than guessed.
 */

import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
import {
  buildJudgeEntries,
  buildJudgePrompt,
  gradeStimulus,
  parseJudgeReply,
  toDetails,
} from "../src/features/activity-centre/recall/grading";

const MODEL = "gemini-2.5-flash";
const SECTIONS = ["pt102-s3", "pt105-s2", "pt134-s3"] as const;
const CONTENT: Record<string, string> = {
  "pt102-s3": "read-recall-colour-pt102-s3.json",
  "pt105-s2": "read-recall-colour-pt105-s2.json",
  "pt134-s3": "read-recall-colour-pt134-s3.json",
};

function arg(name: string, fallback = ""): string {
  const i = process.argv.indexOf(`--${name}`);
  return i === -1 ? fallback : process.argv[i + 1];
}

function geminiKey(): string {
  const env = readFileSync(`${process.env.HOME}/ai-os/.env`, "utf8");
  const line = env.split("\n").find((l) => l.startsWith("GEMINI_API_KEY="));
  const key = line?.slice("GEMINI_API_KEY=".length).trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g, "");
  if (!key) throw new Error("GEMINI_API_KEY is empty in ~/ai-os/.env");
  return key;
}

interface Usage { prompt: number; output: number; total: number }

async function ask(prompt: string, key: string): Promise<{ text: string; usage: Usage }> {
  const res = await fetch(
    `https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/models/${MODEL}:generateContent`,
    {
      method: "POST",
      headers: { "x-goog-api-key": [REDACTED], "Content-Type": "application/json" },
      body: JSON.stringify({
        contents: [{ parts: [{ text: prompt }] }],
        generationConfig: { temperature: 0, responseMimeType: "application/json" },
      }),
    },
  );
  const body = await res.json();
  if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`HTTP ${res.status}: ${JSON.stringify(body).slice(0, 300)}`);
  const m = body.usageMetadata ?? {};
  return {
    text: body.candidates?.[0]?.content?.parts?.[0]?.text ?? "",
    usage: {
      prompt: m.promptTokenCount ?? 0,
      output: m.candidatesTokenCount ?? 0,
      total: m.totalTokenCount ?? 0,
    },
  };
}

async function main() {
  const key = geminiKey();
  const shapedDir = arg("shaped");
----
----
 *   Asking "did she say this?" of a discourse role marks it missed whenever she
 *   carried it structurally, which is a tenth of the key.
 */

export type DetailKind = "content" | "structure" | "modality";

export interface Detail {
  /** 1-based index within the stimulus, stable across the whole judgement. */
  n: number;
  text: string;
  kind: DetailKind;
  /**
   * Which authored line this Detail lives in, when the caller knows.
   *
   * Coverage is still per stimulus (ADR 0013) and this changes no score. It is
   * how a marked Detail finds its way back to the line she was rephrasing when
   * she should have carried it, so the Report Card can say "on line 2 you
   * dropped the quantifier" instead of handing her a list of 21.
   */
  line?: number;
}


export type Band = "at-the-standard" | "nearly-there" | "thin" | "lost-it";

/**
 * Where the bands cut.
 *
 * Only `atTheStandard` is measured: it is the 10th percentile of the 71-run
 * census, so nine correct runs in ten clear it. It is deliberately not the
 * minimum of that census, which is a stimulus whose recording is broken.
 *
 * The other two are PROVISIONAL and marked so. The corpus holds only correct
 * runs, so it can say where good ends and cannot say where "nearly there"
 * stops being nearly. Those move once student runs exist.
 */
export const BAND_FLOOR = {
  atTheStandard: 86,
  nearlyThere: 72,
  thin: 55,
} as const;

export function bandFor(percent: number): Band {
  if (percent >= BAND_FLOOR.atTheStandard) return "at-the-standard";
  if (percent >= BAND_FLOOR.nearlyThere) return "nearly-there";
  if (percent >= BAND_FLOOR.thin) return "thin";
  return "lost-it";
}

  const shapedDir = arg("shaped");
  const limit = Number(arg("limit", "0")) || Infinity;

  const census: Record<string, number> = {};
  const censusFile = JSON.parse(
    readFileSync(`${process.env.HOME}/ai-os/corpus/loophole-online/rrc-sections/coverage-census.json`, "utf8"),
  );
  for (const row of censusFile.stimuli) census[row.slug] = row.coverage;

  interface ContentDetail { text: string }
interface ContentChunk { text: string; details: ContentDetail[] }
interface ContentItem { slug: string; chunks: ContentChunk[] }
interface ShapedRun { slug: string; spoken: string }

type Job = { slug: string; stimulus: string; details: string[]; spoken: string; decoys: string[] };
  const jobs: Job[] = [];

  for (const section of SECTIONS) {
    const content = JSON.parse(
      readFileSync(`src/features/activity-centre/content/${CONTENT[section]}`, "utf8"),
    );
    const shaped = JSON.parse(readFileSync(`${shapedDir}/${section}.json`, "utf8")) as ShapedRun[];
    const spokenBy: Record<string, string> = {};
    for (const run of shaped) spokenBy[run.slug] = run.spoken;

    const items = content.items as ContentItem[];
    items.forEach((item, i) => {
      const details = item.chunks.flatMap((c) => c.details.map((d) => d.text));
      // Decoys come from the NEXT stimulus in the same section, which is the
      // nearest thing in subject matter and therefore the hardest to reject.
      const other = items[(i + 1) % items.length];
      const decoys = other.chunks[0].details.slice(0, 2).map((d) => d.text);
      if (spokenBy[item.slug]) {
        jobs.push({
          slug: item.slug,
          stimulus: item.chunks.map((c) => c.text).join(" "),
          details,
          spoken: spokenBy[item.slug],
          decoys,
        });
      }
    });
  }

  const chosen = jobs.slice(0, limit === Infinity ? jobs.length : limit);
  console.log(`judging ${chosen.length} of ${jobs.length} stimuli on ${MODEL}\n`);

  let promptTokens = 0, outputTokens = 0, decoyFailures = 0;
  const deltas: number[] = [];

  for (const job of chosen) {
    const entries = buildJudgeEntries(toDetails(job.details), job.decoys);
    const prompt = buildJudgePrompt({ stimulus: job.stimulus, entries, spoken: job.spoken });
    try {
      const { text, usage } = await ask(prompt, key);
      promptTokens += usage.prompt;
      outputTokens += usage.output;
      const reply = parseJudgeReply(text, entries.map((e) => e.id));
      const grade = gradeStimulus(entries, reply);
      const expected = census[job.slug];
      const delta = expected === undefined ? NaN : grade.percent - expected;
      if (!Number.isNaN(delta)) deltas.push(delta);
      if (grade.decoysCarried.length) decoyFailures++;
      console.log(
        `${job.slug.padEnd(14)} judge ${String(grade.percent).padStart(3)}%  census ${String(expected ?? "--").padStart(3)}%  ` +
        `delta ${delta >= 0 ? "+" : ""}${Number.isNaN(delta) ? "--" : delta}  ${grade.band}` +
        `${grade.decoysCarried.length ? `  DECOY CARRIED x${grade.decoysCarried.length}` : ""}` +
        `${reply.missingIds.length ? `  unjudged x${reply.missingIds.length}` : ""}`,
      );
    } catch (e) {
      console.log(`${job.slug.padEnd(14)} ERROR ${(e as Error).message.slice(0, 160)}`);
    }
  }

  const mean = deltas.length ? deltas.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / deltas.length : 0;
  const within5 = deltas.filter((d) => Math.abs(d) <= 5).length;
  console.log(`\n--- agreement with the census ---`);
  console.log(`stimuli compared : ${deltas.length}`);
  console.log(`mean delta       : ${mean.toFixed(1)} points (judge minus census)`);
  console.log(`within 5 points  : ${within5}/${deltas.length}`);
  console.log(`decoy failures   : ${decoyFailures}`);
  console.log(`\n--- tokens ---`);
  console.log(`prompt ${promptTokens}, output ${outputTokens}, total ${promptTokens + outputTokens}`);
  console.log(`per stimulus: ~${Math.round((promptTokens + outputTokens) / Math.max(1, chosen.length))} tokens`);
}

main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });

# 0018 — Full Coverage is not 100%

Date: 2026-09-01
Status: accepted
Amended: 2026-09-04 (see "Amendment: a recital wore the top band anyway")
Extends [0012](./0012-the-recall-is-a-recap-from-memory.md).

## Context

ADR 0012 made Coverage the score: how many of a stimulus's authored Details she
carried, as one number per stimulus. It did not say what number is good, and the
obvious reading of a percentage is that 100 is the target.

That reading is measurably false. Joshua's own recordings are the drill done
correctly, and they are the standard the Details were calibrated against. Put
through `student_shaped.py`, which removes the verbatim reads a student never
speaks, **all 71 stimuli of the three built sections were scored Detail by
Detail** — a census, not a sample, of 1,294 judgements:

| | Coverage |
|---|---|
| Mean | 94.7% |
| Median | 95% |
| 10th percentile | 86% |
| Minimum | 68% |
| Maximum | 100% |
| Runs at 100% | 22 of 71 |

He reaches a clean sweep on fewer than a third of them, and the distribution has
a real left tail: two runs below 83%, and one at 68%.

The two lowest are both explained rather than mysterious. PT134.S3.Q16's
recording starts mid-sentence, so part of the stimulus was never spoken at all;
it measures the recording. PT134.S3.Q1 ends in a fill-in-the-blank conclusion
("it would be reasonable for organizations… to _______"), which he answers in
four words, so a whole chunk's Details go uncarried. Excluding those two, the
range is 79 to 100 with a median of 96.

He is not sloppy and the key is not wrong. The calibration method already
settled this: "his own recall drops details, and that is not a defect… his run
is not a ceiling on the key." What is new is the shape of the distribution and
what sits in the misses. Across the census they are, in order of frequency:
**a small specific** — an adjective, a place, a time, a material ("the water is
seawater", "the stalagmites are numerous", "the announcement was earlier
today"); **a quantifier or hedge** ("the devices would go in all cars", "it is
generally so", "this is stated with certainty"); and **a speaker role or
discourse marker** ("Tom is the first speaker", "a lawyer is speaking").

Those are the exact words the drill exists to train. Coverage is measuring the
right thing; the scale is what is wrong.

A second measurement bears on how the judgement is asked. Classifying all 1,294
Details by what they actually assert
(`system/scripts/activity-centre/classify_details.py`) splits them three ways,
not two:

| Kind | Count | Share | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| content | 1,133 | 87.6% | "The commitment is long-standing." |
| structure | 137 | 10.6% | "This is the conclusion." · "This is a contrast." · "An archaeologist is speaking." |

/*
 * Every finished Read, Recall, Colour section that has no complete Report Card.
 *
 *   npx vite-node scripts/rrc-ungraded.ts            # list
 *   npx vite-node scripts/rrc-ungraded.ts -- --grade # list, then re-ask the grader for each
 *
 * The sweeper (`sweep-rrc-grading`) is deliberately scheduled nowhere (ADR
 * 0021), the Report Card asks for a grade twice and then says "still being
 * marked" for good, and a run the grader REFUSES -- no recording, a clock it
 * cannot place, a transcription outage -- produces nothing anywhere that
 * Joshua would see. On 2026-09-08 a student sat on "still being marked" for
 * three and a half hours before she messaged. This is the list she was on.
 *
 * Reads as the admin account. Run it from the repo root.
 */
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";

const REPO = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");
/** Younger than this and the grader is probably still running. */
const SETTLE_MS = 10 * 60 * 1000;

function readEnv(file: string): Record<string, string> {
  const out: Record<string, string> = {};
  if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return out;
  for (const line of fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8").split(/\r?\n/)) {
    const t = line.trim();
    if (!t || t.startsWith("#")) continue;
    const i = t.indexOf("=");
    if (i < 0) continue;
    out[t.slice(0, i).trim()] = t.slice(i + 1).trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g, "");
  }
  return out;
}

async function main() {
  const env = { ...readEnv(path.resolve(REPO, "../../.env")), ...readEnv(path.join(REPO, ".env")) };
  const grade = process.argv.includes("--grade");

---- PROXY
// OpenAI-compatible proxy that forwards traffic to the Lovable AI Gateway.
// Since the Gateway is already OpenAI-compatible, this is a thin pass-through
// that (a) normalizes model ids so unprefixed / legacy names still route to a
// supported Gateway model and (b) preserves streaming + tool calling for the
// 13 internal callers (tutor-chat, ta-chat, study-chat, teacher-operator-chat, …).
//
// Routes:
//   POST /chat/completions  — OpenAI chat completions (streaming + tools)
//   POST /embeddings        — OpenAI embeddings (3072-dim)
//   POST /                  — same as /chat/completions (back-compat)
import { serve } from "https://deno.land/std@0.168.0/http/server.ts";

const corsHeaders = {
  "Access-Control-Allow-Origin": "*",
  "Access-Control-Allow-Headers":
    "authorization, x-client-info, apikey, content-type, lovable-api-key, x-lovable-aig-sdk, x-internal-key",
  "Access-Control-Allow-Methods": "POST, OPTIONS",
};

// Constant-time string comparison — avoids leaking key length/prefix via timing.
function timingSafeEqual(a: string, b: string): boolean {
  const enc = new TextEncoder();
  const ea = enc.encode(a);
  const eb = enc.encode(b);
  if (ea.length !== eb.length) return false;
  let diff = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < ea.length; i++) diff |= ea[i] ^ eb[i];
  return diff === 0;
}


const GOOGLE_BASE = "https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/openai";
const GATEWAY_BASE = "https://ai.gateway.lovable.dev/v1";

// One model for every AI feature on the platform, run on Joshua's own
// GEMINI_API_KEY. Anything a caller asks for is normalised to it, so a legacy
// id left in an old function cannot quietly route somewhere else.
const CHAT_MODEL = "gemini-3.8-flash";
const EMBED_MODEL = "gemini-embedding-001";

function mapChatModel(_model: string | undefined): string {
  return CHAT_MODEL;
}

// Embeddings MUST be 3072-dim to match the DB vector(3072) columns.
function mapEmbedModel(_model: string | undefined): string {
  return EMBED_MODEL;
}

async function forward(
  path: string,
  body: unknown,
  stream: boolean,
): Promise<Response> {
  // Joshua's own Gemini key first: it is the platform's AI account. The Lovable
  // gateway stays as a fallback so nothing goes dark if the key is ever unset.
  const googleKey = Deno.env.get("GEMINI_API_KEY");
  const base = googleKey ? GOOGLE_BASE : GATEWAY_BASE;
  const key = googleKey ?? Deno.env.get("LOVABLE_API_KEY") ?? "";
  const upstream = await fetch(`${base}${path}`, {
    method: "POST",
    headers: {
      "Content-Type": "application/json",
      Authorization: [REDACTED] ${key}`,
      "X-Lovable-AIG-SDK": "gemini-proxy",
    },
    body: JSON.stringify(body),
  });

  if (stream && upstream.ok && upstream.body) {
    return new Response(upstream.body, {
      status: upstream.status,
      headers: {
        ...corsHeaders,
        "Content-Type": upstream.headers.get("Content-Type") ?? "text/event-stream",
        "Cache-Control": "no-cache",
        Connection: "keep-alive",
      },
    });
  }

     * `ungraded` carries the reason the judge could not be reached or could not

    type Graded = StimulusGrade & {
      slug: string;
      /** The assignment's authored position, so a missing response row cannot renumber later rows. */

       * recording playable one stimulus at a time, which ADR 0020 accepted and

       * player and an offset rather than an encoding pipeline.
       */
      spoken?: string;
      window_ms?: { start: number; end: number };
      recall_window_ms?: { start: number; end: number };

      read_aloud_words?: number;
      chunks_read_aloud?: number[];

    });

    /*
     * Which goal this run is scored against, read from the profile and not from
=== INDEX 895-997
     */
    const uncoloured = graded.filter((s) => !s.coloured).length;
    const noRead = graded.filter((s) => !s.readGiven).length;
    const withinTime = totalSeconds > 0 && totalSeconds <= goalMinutes * 60;

    const section = {
      accommodation,
      /*
       * Where the recording is, carried onto the grade.
       *
       * It has always been on the response rows and the Report Card never had
       * it, so the one thing a student most wants after being told she dropped
       * the word "only" -- hearing herself not say it -- needed a second query
       * she had no reason to know to make. ADR 0020's seek was proved exact on
       * the trial run, so a stimulus is a play button and two offsets.
       */
      audio_bucket: (first.audio_bucket as string | null) ?? null,
      audio_path: audioPath,
      goal_minutes: goalMinutes,
      total_seconds: totalSeconds,
      within_time: withinTime,
      within_goal: withinTime && uncoloured === 0 && noRead === 0,
      mean_coverage: meanCoverage,
      stimuli_graded: graded.length,
      stimuli_in_section: expectedSlugs.size || stimuli.length,
      reciting_count: graded.filter((s) => s.reciting).length,
      listing_count: graded.filter((s) => s.listing).length,
      uncoloured_count: uncoloured,
      /*
       * The Read, measured at last.
       *
       * Authored on all 1,294 stimuli, promised to her twice -- the briefing
       * says "this is the part that gets checked" -- and graded by nothing
       * until now. Coverage answers "did you hold it"; this answers "did you
       * understand it", which is the criterion the method is built around.
       */
      read_given_count: graded.filter((s) => s.readGiven).length,
      read_matched_count: graded.filter((s) => s.readMatches).length,
      no_read_count: noRead,
      /*
       * How much of what she said was reading the text aloud.
       *
       * Cut before judging, because the standard the Details were calibrated
       * against has it cut too. Recorded because a student who reads the whole
       * section aloud is doing a different drill from the one she thinks she is
       * doing, and nothing else in the product would ever say so.
       */
      read_aloud_words: stimuli.reduce((a, s) => a + (s.read_aloud_words ?? 0), 0),
      chunks_read_aloud: stimuli.reduce((a, s) => a + (s.chunks_read_aloud?.length ?? 0), 0),
      // Measured from the audio, not from the judge, so an ungraded stimulus
      // still has an honest reading here.
      worst_dead_air_seconds: Math.max(0, ...stimuli.map((s) => s.dead_air_seconds)),
      dead_air_threshold_seconds: DEAD_AIR_SECONDS,
      decoy_failures: graded.filter((s) => s.decoysCarried.length > 0).length,
      /*
       * Whether every stimulus in this section actually got a judgement.
       *
       * The one flag the sweeper reads. A partial grade is written rather than
       * thrown away -- the recording, the upload and the transcription behind
       * it all worked -- but it must not pass for a finished one, or the run
       * that needs re-driving is the one run nothing will ever look at again.
       */
      complete: ungraded.length === 0,
      ungraded_count: ungraded.length,
      clock,
    };

    const { error: wErr } = await admin.from("activity_section_grades").upsert(
      {
        assignment_id: assignmentId,
        attempt_no: attempt,
        student_id: assignment.student_id,
        source_section: assignment.source_section,
        transcript_engine: ASR_ENGINE,
        transcript_text: words.map((w) => w.word).join(" "),
        grader_version: GRADER_VERSION,
        stimuli,
        section,
        graded_at: new Date().toISOString(),
      },
      { onConflict: "assignment_id,attempt_no" },
    );
    if (wErr) throw wErr;

    // Advisory, and written last: nothing derives trust from it (ADR 0019).
    await admin
      .from("activity_assignments")
      .update({ status: "completed", completed_at: new Date().toISOString() })
      .eq("id", assignmentId);

    return json({
      status: section.complete ? "graded" : "graded-partial",
      attempt,
      stimuli: stimuli.length,
      section,
    });
  } catch (e) {
    console.error("[grade-rrc-section]", e);
    return json({ error: (e as Error).message }, 500);
  } finally {
    if (release) await release();
  }
});

serve(async (req) => {
  if (req.method === "OPTIONS") return new Response(null, { headers: corsHeaders });

  /*
   * The claim this request holds on an Attempt's grading, if any.
   *
   * Released in `finally`, whatever happens after it is taken: a grading that
   * returns early, throws, or finishes all let go of the Attempt, and one that
   * dies without reaching `finally` is let go of by the claim going stale.
   */

    const auth = req.headers.get("Authorization");
    if (!auth) return json({ error: "unauthorized" }, 401);

    const { assignmentId, force, attempt: askedFor, diagnose } = await req.json();
    if (!assignmentId && !diagnose) return json({ error: "assignmentId is required" }, 400);

    const admin = createClient(
      Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_URL")!,
      Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY")!,
    );

    /*
     * Who is asking, and one caller that is not a person.
     *
     * The sweeper re-drives runs whose student has long since closed the tab,
     * so it has no user to present. It calls with the service role key, which
     * never leaves the server and already grants everything the READS below
     * do -- so accepting it here widens nothing, it just names the caller the
     * function already trusts. Compared in constant time and checked BEFORE
     * `getUser`, which returns no user for a service-role token and would
     * otherwise reject the sweeper as unauthorized.

    const serviceKey = Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY")!;

// --- Reading the answer ------------------------------------------------------

export interface JudgeReply {
  verdicts: Record<number, boolean>;
  reciting: boolean;
  recitingReason: string;
  listing: boolean;
  listingReason: string;
  coloured: boolean;
  colourSpecific: boolean;
  /**
   * The Read: whether she gave one at all, and whether it was the right move.
   *
   * Two flags rather than one, because they fail differently and the coaching
   * differs. No Read at all is a habit -- she stopped at the retelling. A Read
   * that missed the move is comprehension, and it is the number that predicts
   * a score.
   */
  readGiven: boolean;
  readMatches: boolean;
  readReason: string;
  /** Ids expected but absent from the reply. Counted as not carried. */
  missingIds: number[];
  /** At most three plain-words phrases naming what she dropped that mattered. */
  bigMisses: string[];
  /** Per-line judgement, keyed by line number. Absent when lines were not sent. */
  lineVerdicts: Record<number, { verdict: LineVerdict; note: string }>;
  /** The same judgement for the whole-stimulus recall, when it was sent. */
  recallVerdict?: { verdict: LineVerdict; note: string };
}

export type LineVerdict = "strong" | "partial" | "thin" | "skipped";

const LINE_VERDICTS: LineVerdict[] = ["strong", "partial", "thin", "skipped"];

function toLineVerdict(value: unknown): LineVerdict {
  const v = String(value ?? "").toLowerCase() as LineVerdict;
  // Anything unrecognised is "thin" rather than "strong": a judge that garbles
  // the field must not be able to award a line she may never have spoken.
  return LINE_VERDICTS.includes(v) ? v : "thin";
}


export class JudgeReplyError extends Error {}

/**
 * Pull the JSON out of whatever the model actually sent.
 *
 * Told not to use a code fence, models use one anyway, and some prepend a
 * sentence. Rather than trusting the instruction this finds the outermost
 * balanced object. A reply that is not parseable is an error and never a zero:
 * a grade of 0% and a grade we failed to read are different facts, and only one
 * of them is about her.
 */
export function extractJson(raw: string): unknown {
  const text = raw.trim().replace(/^```(?:json)?/i, "").replace(/```$/, "").trim();
  const start = text.indexOf("{");
  if (start === -1) throw new JudgeReplyError("no JSON object in the reply");
  let depth = 0;
  let inString = false;
  let escaped = false;
  for (let i = start; i < text.length; i++) {
    const c = text[i];
    if (inString) {
      if (escaped) escaped = false;
      else if (c === "\\") escaped = true;
      else if (c === '"') inString = false;
      continue;
    }
    if (c === '"') inString = true;
    else if (c === "{") depth++;
    else if (c === "}" && --depth === 0) {
      try {
        return JSON.parse(text.slice(start, i + 1));
      } catch (e) {
        throw new JudgeReplyError(`unparseable JSON: ${(e as Error).message}`);
      }
    }
  }
  throw new JudgeReplyError("JSON object never closed");
}

export function parseJudgeReply(raw: string, expectedIds: number[]): JudgeReply {
  const obj = extractJson(raw) as Record<string, unknown>;
  const rows = obj.verdicts;
  if (!Array.isArray(rows)) throw new JudgeReplyError("reply has no verdicts array");

  const verdicts: Record<number, boolean> = {};
  for (const row of rows) {
    const r = row as Record<string, unknown>;
    const id = typeof r.id === "number" ? r.id : Number(r.id);
    if (!Number.isFinite(id)) continue;
    verdicts[id] = r.carried === true;
  }
  const missingIds = expectedIds.filter((id) => !(id in verdicts));
  const flag = (k: string) => obj[k] === true;
  const reason = (k: string) => (typeof obj[k] === "string" ? (obj[k] as string) : "");

  const lineVerdicts: Record<number, { verdict: LineVerdict; note: string }> = {};
  if (Array.isArray(obj.lines)) {
    for (const row of obj.lines as Record<string, unknown>[]) {
      const n = Number(row?.n);
      if (!Number.isFinite(n)) continue;
      lineVerdicts[n] = {
        verdict: toLineVerdict(row.verdict),
        note: typeof row.note === "string" ? row.note : "",
      };
    }
  }
  const recallRow = obj.recall as Record<string, unknown> | undefined;

  return {
    verdicts,
    reciting: flag("reciting"),
    recitingReason: reason("reciting_reason"),
    listing: flag("listing"),
    listingReason: reason("listing_reason"),
    // Absent is treated as "no colour" rather than as colour, so a judge that
    // forgets the field cannot silently award it.
    coloured: flag("coloured"),
    colourSpecific: flag("colour_specific"),
    // Absent is "no Read", for the same reason as colour: a judge that forgets
    // the field must not be able to award it, and a stimulus judged before The
    // Read was graded at all is honestly reported as not having one.
    readGiven: flag("read_given"),
    readMatches: flag("read_matches"),
    readReason: reason("read_reason"),
    missingIds,
    bigMisses: Array.isArray(obj.big_misses)
      ? (obj.big_misses as unknown[])
          .filter((m): m is string => typeof m === "string" && m.trim() !== "")
          .slice(0, 3)
      : [],
    lineVerdicts,
    recallVerdict: recallRow
      ? { verdict: toLineVerdict(recallRow.verdict), note: typeof recallRow.note === "string" ? recallRow.note : "" }
      : undefined,
  };
}

export interface LineGrade {

  /** What she said for it. Empty means she said nothing there. */

  startMs?: number;
  endMs?: number;
  verdict: LineVerdict;
  note: string;
  /** Details authored under this line that she carried, and those she did not. */
  carried: string[];
  dropped: string[];
}

export interface StimulusGrade {
  carried: number;
  total: number;
  /** Whole-number percent over the stimulus's OWN Details. */
  percent: number;
  band: Band;
  missed: string[];
  /** Decoys the judge said she carried. Any at all discredits the run. */

  listing: boolean;

  lines: LineGrade[];
  /** The whole-stimulus recall she gave after the text disappeared. */
  recall?: { verdict: LineVerdict; note: string };
  /** At most three plain-words phrases naming what she dropped that mattered. */
  bigMisses: string[];
}

=== TYPES
/**
 * One stimulus on the Report Card.
 *
 * Mirrors what `grade-rrc-section` writes. `percent` is Coverage over that
 * stimulus's own Details; `band` is what the student is actually shown, because
 * a correct run is not 100% and a raw percentage against an implied maximum
 * misreports it (ADR 0018).
 */
export interface SectionStimulusGrade {
  slug: string;
  /** Authored position in the section. Absent on grades written before ADR 0021's missing-row amendment. */
  item_order?: number;
  carried: number;
  total: number;
  percent: number;
  band: "at-the-standard" | "nearly-there" | "thin" | "lost-it";
  /** The Details she did not carry, as authored. */
  missed: string[];
  /** Details from another stimulus the judge wrongly credited. Any at all makes this stimulus suspect. */
  decoysCarried: string[];
  /** Details the judge returned no verdict for. Counted as not carried. */
  unjudged: string[];
  reciting: boolean;
  listing: boolean;
  coloured: boolean;
  colourSpecific: boolean;
  dead_air_seconds: number;
  /** Did she give a Read at all, and was it the move the key names. */
  readGiven?: boolean;
  readMatches?: boolean;
  readReason?: string;
  /** L, I, C or R. Authored on the stimulus, not inferred from her run. */
  the_read_type?: string;
  /** Words cut as read-aloud before judging, and which chunks she read. */
  read_aloud_words?: number;
  chunks_read_aloud?: number[];
  /** What the machine heard for this stimulus, after the reads were cut. */
  spoken?: string;
  /** Where this stimulus sits in the recording, for a Clip. */
  window_ms?: { start: number; end: number };
  recall_window_ms?: { start: number; end: number };
  /**
   * Her run cut into the lines she was rephrasing, in authored order.
   *
   * Feedback only -- `percent` above is the same number it always was, scored
   * over the whole stimulus. Absent on grades written before line attribution
   * existed, so every reader has to treat it as optional.
   */
  lines?: SectionLineGrade[];
  /** The whole-stimulus recall she gave once the text disappeared. */
  recall?: { verdict: SectionLineVerdict; note: string };
  /** At most three plain-words phrases naming what she dropped that mattered. */
  bigMisses?: string[];
  /** Why the judge returned nothing. Absent on every stimulus that was judged. */
  ungraded?: string;
}

export type SectionLineVerdict = "strong" | "partial" | "thin" | "skipped";

export interface SectionLineGrade {
  /** 1-based line number within the stimulus. */
  n: number;
  text: string;
  /** What she said for that line. Empty means she said nothing there. */
  spoken: string;
  startMs?: number;
  endMs?: number;
  verdict: SectionLineVerdict;
  note: string;
  carried: string[];
  dropped: string[];
}


export interface SectionGradeSummary {
  accommodation: string;
  /** Where the recording lives, so a Clip is a seek rather than a second query. */
  audio_bucket?: string | null;
  audio_path?: string | null;
  goal_minutes: number;
  total_seconds: number;
  /** Inside the clock, ignoring the method's condition on it. */
  within_time?: boolean;
  /** Inside the clock AND with every stimulus coloured and given a Read. */
  within_goal: boolean;
  mean_coverage: number;
  stimuli_graded: number;
  /** Number of stimuli in the assignment snapshot, including any whose response row never arrived. */
  stimuli_in_section?: number;
  reciting_count: number;
  listing_count: number;
  uncoloured_count: number;
  read_given_count?: number;
  read_matched_count?: number;
  no_read_count?: number;
  read_aloud_words?: number;
  chunks_read_aloud?: number;
  ungraded_count?: number;
  complete?: boolean;
  worst_dead_air_seconds: number;
  dead_air_threshold_seconds: number;
  decoy_failures: number;
  clock: Record<string, number>;
}

/**
 * Just enough of a Report Card to list it.
 *
 * The Attempt list on the section library needs a date and one number per
 * sitting; the grade itself carries a full transcript and a judgement per
 * stimulus, and pulling every one of those to draw a line of grey text would
 * make opening the library the heaviest read in the product.
 */
export interface SectionGradeStub {
  assignment_id: string;
  attempt_no: number;

  section: SectionGradeSummary;

}

export interface SectionGrade {
  assignment_id: string;

=== NEXTSTEP
import { missKind, MISS_LABEL, type MissKind } from "./misses";
import type { SectionGradeSummary, SectionStimulusGrade } from "../api/types";

/**
 * One sentence saying what to do tomorrow.
 *
 * The Report Card reports and does not advise. The audit asked the plain
 * question "would she know what to do next?" and the answer was no: every
 * number on the card is a description of what happened, and a student who
 * reads it learns that she held 94% and nothing about what to change.
 *
 * Everything this needs was already stored and shown as an observation. The
 * habits row says "3 with no reaction of your own"; it does not say that the
 * reaction is the half of the drill that makes the memory stick. That gap
 * between the count and the instruction is all this closes.
 *
 * ## The rules it obeys
 *
 * **Derived only from stored fields.** Nothing here infers, estimates or
 * encourages. If the run does not say something clearly, this says nothing.
 *
 * **Exactly one, or none.** Two pieces of advice are a list, and a list is what
 * a student ignores. The order below is a real ranking rather than a fallback
 * chain: the method beats the content, because a run done the wrong way cannot
 * be fixed by remembering harder.
 *
 * **Never a generic encouragement.** "Keep going" is the failure mode this is
 * written against. A run with nothing to say about it gets nothing.
 */

/**
 * How much of a run a fault has to touch before it is the thing to work on.
 *
 * A third. Below that it is one bad stimulus, which the row for that stimulus
 * already says, and hoisting it to the top of the card overstates it.
 */
const SHARE = 1 / 3;

/**
 * How many Details of one kind she has to have dropped before the pattern is a
 * pattern, and how much of her dropping it has to account for.
 *
 * A single run is a small sample. Three misses of one kind, holding a third of
 * everything she dropped, is the least that separates a habit from an accident.
 */
const KIND_FLOOR = 3;

export function nextStep(
  section: SectionGradeSummary,
  stimuli: SectionStimulusGrade[],
): string | null {
  const graded = stimuli.length;
  if (graded === 0) return null;
  const enough = (n: number) => n / graded >= SHARE;

  /*
   * The Read first, because it is the criterion the method is built around.
   *
   * Coverage answers "did you hold it" and The Read answers "did you understand
   * it". A student who says every detail back and never says what the argument
   * was doing has done a memory exercise, and this drill is not one.
   */
  const noRead = section.no_read_count ?? 0;
  if (noRead > 0 && enough(noRead)) {
    return `You gave no Read at all on ${noRead} of them. Before you move on from a stimulus, say in one line what the argument was doing. That is the part that shows up in your score.`;
  }

  /*
   * Then the three ways of running it that make the number meaningless. Order
   * among them is by how far each one is from the drill: saying it back word
   * for word is not recall at all, a list is recall without understanding, and
   * no reaction is understanding without anything to hold it.
   */
  if (section.reciting_count > 0 && enough(section.reciting_count)) {
    return `On ${section.reciting_count} of them you said it back close to word for word. Cover the text and put it in your own words, even clumsy ones. Your own words are what the memory holds onto.`;
  }
  if (section.listing_count > 0 && enough(section.listing_count)) {
    return `On ${section.listing_count} of them you told it as a list of facts. Say what the argument was trying to do with those facts, so the order comes back with them.`;
  }
  if (section.uncoloured_count > 0 && enough(section.uncoloured_count)) {
    return `On ${section.uncoloured_count} of them you added no reaction of your own. Say one thing you think about the claim as you go, even a doubt. It is what makes it stick.`;
  }

  /*
   * Then what she drops, when there is enough of one kind to call it a pattern.
   *
   * Counted rather than shared, because one card cannot compute the share: it
   * stores the Details she missed and not the ones she carried. A raw count
   * would name "Plain facts" on every card ever printed, since plain facts
   * outnumber everything else about eight to one, so the residual bucket is
   * excluded and the leader has to hold a third of her misses on its own.
   */
  const dropped = new Map<MissKind, number>();
  for (const s of stimuli) {
    for (const text of s.missed) dropped.set(missKind(text), (dropped.get(missKind(text)) ?? 0) + 1);
  }
  const total = [...dropped.values()].reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0);
  const [kind, count] = [...dropped.entries()]
    .filter(([k]) => k !== "claim")
    .sort((a, b) => b[1] - a[1])[0] ?? [null, 0];
  if (kind && count >= KIND_FLOOR && count / total >= SHARE) {
    return `${MISS_LABEL[kind]} is what went missing most: ${count} of the ${total} details you dropped. Say those out loud on purpose as you read the chunk.`;
  }

  // Nothing this run said clearly enough to act on. Better than a sentence she
  // would learn to skip.
  return null;
}

=== CARD 1-330
import { useCallback, useEffect, useMemo, useRef, useState } from "react";
import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";
import { bandFor } from "../recall/grading";
import { nextStep } from "../recall/nextStep";
import { listModelRuns, signSectionRecording } from "../api/activitiesClient";
import { raiseDispute } from "../recall/disputes";
import type { ModelRun } from "../api/activitiesClient";
import type { SectionGrade, SectionStimulusGrade } from "../api/types";

/**
 * What a finished Read, Recall, Colour section produces.
 *
 * Every stimulus graded on its own merits, showing what she missed and what she
 * got perfect. Three rules from the decisions behind it shape what is on
 * screen:
 *
 * - **A band, not a percentage.** A correct run by the author of the standard
 *   sits at a median of 95% and clears 86% nine times in ten, so a raw score
 *   against an implied 100 tells a student she failed at something she did
 *   properly (ADR 0018). This card's own docstring said that and then printed
 *   the percentage as the hero in 48px type with no band at all. The band leads
 *   now; the number is under it, because "what did I actually get" is a fair
 *   question that just does not go first.
 * - **The band answers "was this good"; the missed Details answer "what did I
 *   drop".** Both are shown even at the standard, which is what stops the top
 *   band reading as nothing left to learn.
 * - **Everything measured is either shown or deliberately not.** The grader
 *   computed her transcript, her dead air, whether each reaction was specific,
 *   whether the judge credited words she never said, and how many stimuli it
 *   could not mark, and rendered none of it. A number a student cannot
 *   interrogate is a number she has to take on faith.
 *
 * Internal identifiers never appear. She sees "Stimulus 4", never the capture's
 * own slug, and never the decoy or grader-version machinery.
 */

interface SectionReportCardProps {
  grade: SectionGrade;
  onDone?: () => void;
  /** Injected in tests and in the lab, which has no database behind it. */
  loadModelRuns?: (sourceSection: string) => Promise<Record<string, ModelRun>>;
}

const BAND: Record<SectionStimulusGrade["band"], { label: string; cls: string; dot: string }> = {
  "at-the-standard": {
    label: "At the standard",
    cls: "text-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald-strong))]",
    dot: "bg-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald-strong))]",
  },
  "nearly-there": { label: "Nearly there", cls: "text-[hsl(38_92%_34%)]", dot: "bg-[hsl(38_92%_44%)]" },
  thin: { label: "Thin", cls: "text-[hsl(24_85%_42%)]", dot: "bg-[hsl(24_85%_50%)]" },
  "lost-it": { label: "Worth another read", cls: "text-[hsl(350_80%_45%)]", dot: "bg-[hsl(350_80%_55%)]" },
};

/**
 * What a recited stimulus wears instead of a band.
 *
 * ADR 0018 says coverage reached by reciting is a failure, and the row said the
 * opposite. In the audit's run, stimulus 3 was read out verbatim during The
 * Recall, was correctly flagged, and still sat on the card as "At the standard,
 * 20 of 20" in green. The recital sentence was inside the expanded row, which
 * is the half she has to go looking for, and the band is the half she reads.
 *
 * Not a fifth band, because it is not a worse score -- a recital can carry every
 * detail, and that is exactly the problem. It is the statement that the number
 * beside it did not come from memory, so it is neutral rather than red: the
 * point is the method, not a mark against her.
 */
const RECITED = {
  label: "Said back word for word",
  cls: "text-muted-foreground",
  dot: "bg-muted-foreground",
};

function minutes(seconds: number): string {
  const m = Math.floor(seconds / 60);
  const s = Math.round(seconds % 60);
  return `${m}:${String(s).padStart(2, "0")}`;
}

/**
 * Play one stimulus's stretch of the section recording.
 *
 * The whole run is one file and the trial proved a seek into it lands with zero
 * error, so a Clip is a start offset, an end offset and one audio element.
 * Nothing is cut server-side and nothing is downloaded twice.
 *
 * The link is signed on first use rather than on mount: most Report Cards are
 * read without anyone pressing play, and a signed URL fetched for every card
 * that opens is a request per view for a file nobody asked for.
 */
function useClip() {
  const audio = useRef<HTMLAudioElement | null>(null);
  const stopAt = useRef<number | null>(null);
  const [playing, setPlaying] = useState<string | null>(null);
  /** One signed link per file, made on first use and reused after. */
  const urls = useRef<Map<string, Promise<string | null>>>(new Map());

  useEffect(() => {
    const el = audio.current;
    return () => {
      el?.pause();
    };
  }, []);

  const stop = useCallback(() => {
    audio.current?.pause();
    stopAt.current = null;
    setPlaying(null);
  }, []);

  const play = useCallback(
    async (key: string, file: { bucket: string; path: string }, startMs = 0, endMs?: number) => {
      if (playing === key) {
        stop();
        return;
      }
      const id = `${file.bucket}/${file.path}`;
      if (!urls.current.has(id)) urls.current.set(id, signSectionRecording(file.bucket, file.path));
      const src = await urls.current.get(id)!;
      if (!src) return;
      let el = audio.current;
      if (!el) {
        el = new Audio();
        el.preload = "none";
        // The end of a Clip is a time, not an event the element knows about, so
        // it is watched for rather than waited on.
        el.addEventListener("timeupdate", () => {
          if (stopAt.current !== null && el!.currentTime >= stopAt.current) {
            el!.pause();
            stopAt.current = null;
            setPlaying(null);
          }
        });
        el.addEventListener("ended", () => setPlaying(null));
        audio.current = el;
      }
      if (el.src !== src) el.src = src;
      stopAt.current = endMs === undefined ? null : endMs / 1000;
      el.currentTime = startMs / 1000;
      setPlaying(key);
      try {
        await el.play();
      } catch {
        // Autoplay policy, or a file that will not load. The rest of the card is
        // the Report Card; this is the part that needs a recording to exist.
        setPlaying(null);
      }
    },
    [playing, stop],
  );

  return { play, playing };
}

/**
 * The same stimulus, done properly.
 *
 * Written first: every stimulus of every built section has a walkthrough, the
 * run cut into the verbatim read with the recall in prose beneath it. Most also
 * have the recording it was cut from, and nine never will, because the source
 * never recorded them and they were authored against the measured standard
 * instead. She reads by default and listens if she wants to, which is the right
 * way round -- a recording cannot be skimmed, searched, or sat with one line at
 * a time while she works out where her own recall lost a quantifier.
 *
 * Asked for only once a Report Card is on screen, which is also the only
 * condition under which the database will return them: a student who could
 * reach the model run for a section she has not sat could listen to a correct
 * recall and then "recall" it. After her own grade exists it can no longer help
 * her and can only teach her.
 */
function useModelRuns(
  sourceSection: string,
  load: (sourceSection: string) => Promise<Record<string, ModelRun>>,
) {
  const [runs, setRuns] = useState<Record<string, ModelRun>>({});
  useEffect(() => {
    let cancelled = false;
    void (async () => {
      try {
        const found = await load(sourceSection);
        if (!cancelled) setRuns(found);
      } catch {
        // No model runs is the ordinary answer for a section nobody has
        // finished, and the card simply does not offer the button.
      }
    })();
    return () => {
      cancelled = true;
    };
  }, [sourceSection, load]);
  return runs;
}

/**
 * The model run as something she can read.
 *
 * Rendered from the walkthrough's own shape rather than through a markdown
 * library: the document is exactly two kinds of line, a `> ` quote holding the
 * chunk as it was read aloud and a paragraph holding the recall of it, and
 * parsing those two directly means no dependency and nothing that can render
 * markup the vault put in a file. Anything unexpected falls through as a
 * paragraph, which is the safe direction.
 *
 * Shown open, under her own words, so the comparison is the default rather than
 * a click. "You dropped the word only" is a correction; her sentence sitting
 * above his is the lesson.
 */
function Walkthrough({ md }: { md: string }) {
  const lines = md.split("\n").filter((l) => l.trim());
  return (
    <div className="mt-5 rounded-xl border border-border bg-background/40 p-4">
      <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-wide text-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald-strong))]">
        Done right
      </p>
      <div className="mt-3 space-y-3">
        {lines.map((line, i) =>
          line.startsWith("> ") ? (
            <p
              key={i}
              className="border-l-2 border-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald-strong))] pl-3 text-sm italic leading-relaxed text-foreground"
            >
              {line.slice(2)}
            </p>
          ) : (
            <p key={i} className="text-sm leading-relaxed text-muted-foreground">
              {line}
            </p>
          ),
        )}
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

export function SectionReportCard({
  grade,
  onDone,
  loadModelRuns = listModelRuns,
}: SectionReportCardProps) {
  const [openStimulus, setOpenStimulus] = useState<number | null>(null);
  const { section, stimuli } = grade;
  const clip = useClip();
  const modelRuns = useModelRuns(grade.source_section, loadModelRuns);
  /** Her own recording, when the upload survived. */
  const hers =
    section.audio_bucket && section.audio_path
      ? { bucket: section.audio_bucket, path: section.audio_path }
      : null;

  const overall = useMemo(() => {
    const perfect = stimuli.filter((s) => s.percent === 100).length;
    const atStandard = stimuli.filter((s) => s.band === "at-the-standard").length;
    /*
     * The run's own band, which the card was missing entirely.
     *
     * Computed from the mean the same way a stimulus's is from its own percent,
     * so the headline and the rows cannot disagree about what 86 means.
     */
    const band = BAND[bandFor(section.mean_coverage)];
    /*
     * Whether the marking of this run can be trusted, in her terms.
     *
     * Two measurements, neither of which is about her. `decoy_failures` counts
     * stimuli where the judge credited a Detail belonging to a different
     * stimulus, which means it credited words she never said. `ungraded_count`
     * counts stimuli the judge never answered for, which are otherwise counted
     * as missed -- scoring her for our outage.
     */
    const suspect = (section.decoy_failures ?? 0) > 0;
    const unmarked = section.ungraded_count ?? 0;
    /** Whether The Read was measured at all. Older grades predate it. */
    const readMeasured = section.read_given_count !== undefined;
    /**
     * New grades carry both the assignment's size and each row's authored
     * position. Older grades carry neither, so they keep their historical
     * array-order rendering rather than having a section shape invented for
     * them from a slug.
     */
    const hasStoredPositions = stimuli.every((s) => s.item_order !== undefined);
    const sectionSize = hasStoredPositions ? section.stimuli_in_section : undefined;
    const missingRows = sectionSize === undefined ? 0 : Math.max(0, sectionSize - stimuli.length);

    /*
     * What she would change next time, in her own terms.
     *
     * Every one of these was measured by the grader and some were shown
     * nowhere. They are collected rather than rendered inline so a run with
     * nothing to say about it shows nothing at all, instead of an empty heading.
     */
    const notes: string[] = [];
    if (section.reciting_count > 0)
      notes.push(`${section.reciting_count} said back close to word for word`);
    if (section.listing_count > 0)
      notes.push(`${section.listing_count} told as a list rather than an argument`);
    if (section.uncoloured_count > 0)
      notes.push(`${section.uncoloured_count} with no reaction of your own`);
    if (section.worst_dead_air_seconds >= section.dead_air_threshold_seconds)
      notes.push(`Longest freeze inside a recall: ${section.worst_dead_air_seconds}s`);
    if ((section.chunks_read_aloud ?? 0) > 0)
      notes.push(
        `You read ${section.chunks_read_aloud} of them out loud before recalling. That is not marked against you, and reading silently is the harder, better version.`,
      );

    return {
      perfect,
      atStandard,
      band,
      next: nextStep(section, stimuli),
      suspect,
      unmarked,
      readMeasured,
      notes,
      sectionSize,
      missingRows,
    };
  }, [stimuli, section]);

  return (
    <div className="mx-auto max-w-3xl">
      <div className="rounded-[28px] border border-border bg-[hsl(var(--ac-surface))] p-8 shadow-[0_2px_24px_rgba(30,24,16,0.06)] md:p-12">
        <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.2em] text-muted-foreground">
          {grade.attempt_no > 1 ? `Report Card · Attempt ${grade.attempt_no}` : "Report Card"}
        </p>

        {/* The band leads. The number is available and does not go first. */}
        <p className={cn("ac-display mt-3 text-5xl", overall.band.cls)}>{overall.band.label}</p>
        <p className="mt-2 text-sm text-muted-foreground">

## assistant

Reading the rest of the Report Card and the supporting files (client band mirror, parity test, deploy route, read-stripping helper) before writing any code.

          {overall.sectionSize !== undefined
            ? `${section.stimuli_graded} of ${overall.sectionSize} stimuli, marked`
            : overall.readMeasured
            ? `${section.stimuli_graded} ${section.stimuli_graded === 1 ? "stimulus" : "stimuli"}, marked`
            : `${section.mean_coverage}% of the details carried, across ${section.stimuli_graded} ${

        {overall.next && (

          </p>
        )}

        {/*
          * The two numbers, side by side.
          *
          * Coverage answers "did you hold it". The Read answers "did you

          * It was authored on every stimulus, promised to her in the briefing,
          * and measured by nothing until now. Putting them together is the
          * whole point: one of them is memory and the other is the one that
          * predicts a score, and a card showing only the first says the drill
          * is about memory.
          */}
        {overall.readMeasured && (
          <div className="mt-6 grid gap-6 border-t border-border pt-6 sm:grid-cols-2">
            <div>
              <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.16em] text-muted-foreground">
                Held
              </p>
              <p className="ac-display mt-1 text-3xl text-foreground">{section.mean_coverage}%</p>
              <p className="mt-1 text-xs text-muted-foreground">of the details, said back</p>
            </div>
            <div>
              <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.16em] text-muted-foreground">
                Understood
              </p>
              <p className="ac-display mt-1 text-3xl text-foreground">
                {section.read_matched_count} of {section.stimuli_graded}
              </p>
              <p className="mt-1 text-xs text-muted-foreground">
                {(section.no_read_count ?? 0) > 0
                  ? `Reads that found the move. You gave none at all on ${section.no_read_count}.`
                  : "Reads that found the move"}
              </p>
            </div>
          </div>
        )}

        {/*
          * The clock, and the condition on it.
          *
          * The goal counts only if every stimulus was recalled, coloured and
          * given a Read. Collapsing that into one boolean is what let a student
          * skip the colouring, finish early and be told she made the goal, so
          * the qualifier sits directly under the time rather than beside it in
          * a wrapped row of unrelated remarks.
          */}
        <div className="mt-6 border-t border-border pt-6">
          <p
            className={cn(
              "text-sm",
              section.within_goal ? "text-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald-strong))]" : "text-foreground",
            )}
          >
            {minutes(section.total_seconds)} against a {section.goal_minutes} minute goal
          </p>
          {section.within_time && !section.within_goal && (
            <p className="mt-1 text-xs text-muted-foreground">
              Inside the time, but the goal only counts with every stimulus coloured and given a
              Read.
            </p>
          )}
          {/*
            * Over the goal, which used to get nothing at all.
            *
            * The qualifier above fires only when she is inside the time and
            * short of the goal, so going over rendered as plain uncoloured
            * text with no comment on it: the one case most in need of a word.
            * Not a rebuke. Early runs are slow by design and the briefing
            * already says to go slower while it is new, so this says what the
            * time is for rather than that she was late.
            */}
          {section.within_time === false && (
            <p className="mt-1 text-xs text-muted-foreground">
              Over the goal, which early runs are meant to be. The goal is what the drill costs once
              it is a habit, not a limit on today.
            </p>
          )}
        </div>

        {/*
          * The habits, as a list rather than a wrapped row.
          *
          * These are five different observations about how she ran the section
          * and they were laid out as inline spans, so they read as one run-on
          * sentence in amber. One per line, and the section is absent entirely
          * when there is nothing to say, which is what makes a clean run look
          * clean.
          */}
        {overall.notes.length > 0 && (
          <div className="mt-6 border-t border-border pt-6">
            <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.16em] text-muted-foreground">
              Worth working on
            </p>
            <ul className="mt-3 space-y-1.5">
              {overall.notes.map((note) => (
                <li key={note} className="text-sm text-foreground">
                  {note}
                </li>
              ))}
            </ul>
          </div>
        )}

        {/*
          * When the marking itself is in doubt, she is told before the number.
          *
          * Both of these are about us. A stimulus the judge never answered for
          * is counted as missed, which scores her for our outage, and she is
          * the only person in a position to notice the score looks wrong.
          */}
        {(overall.suspect || overall.unmarked > 0) && (
          <div className="mt-6 rounded-2xl border border-border bg-[hsl(var(--muted))] px-4 py-3 text-sm text-muted-foreground">
            {overall.unmarked > 0 && (
              <p>
                {overall.unmarked} {overall.unmarked === 1 ? "stimulus" : "stimuli"} could not be
                marked, so {overall.unmarked === 1 ? "it counts" : "they count"} as nothing carried.
                That is ours, not yours. Come back later and it will have been marked properly.
              </p>
            )}
            {overall.suspect && (
              <p className={overall.unmarked > 0 ? "mt-2" : undefined}>
                Our own check on this marking came back unhappy, so treat the number on one or two
                of these lightly. Joshua can see the same thing.
              </p>
            )}
          </div>
        )}
      </div>

      <ol className="mt-6 space-y-3">
        {stimuli.map((s, i) => {
          // A recital does not get a success band, whatever it carried.
          const band = s.reciting ? RECITED : BAND[s.band];
          const open = openStimulus === i;

          const key = `s${i}`;
          const model = modelRuns[s.slug];
          return (
            <li key={s.slug} className="rounded-2xl border border-border bg-[hsl(var(--ac-surface))]">

                type="button"
                className="flex w-full items-center gap-4 px-5 py-4 text-left"
                onClick={() => setOpenStimulus(open ? null : i)}
                aria-expanded={open}
              >
                <span className={cn("h-2 w-2 shrink-0 rounded-full", band.dot)} aria-hidden />
                <span className="w-24 shrink-0 text-sm text-muted-foreground">
                  Stimulus {s.item_order ?? i + 1}
                </span>
                <span className={cn("text-sm font-medium", band.cls)}>{band.label}</span>
                <span className="ml-auto text-sm tabular-nums text-muted-foreground">
                  {s.carried} of {s.total}
                </span>
              </button>

              {open && (
                <div className="border-t border-border px-5 py-4">
                  {s.ungraded ? (
                    <p className="text-sm text-muted-foreground">
                      This one could not be marked, so it is showing as nothing carried. That is
                      ours, not yours.
                    </p>
                  ) : s.missed.length === 0 ? (
                    /* The same contradiction as the band, one level down. A
                       recital carries everything by construction, so
                       congratulating it in green sits directly above the

                    <p
