                        s.reciting ? "text-muted-foreground" : "text-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald-strong))]",
                      )}
                    >
                      {s.reciting
                        ? "Every detail is in there, which is what reciting does rather than what you did."
                        : "You carried every detail in this one."}
                    </p>
                  ) : (
                    <>
                      <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.16em] text-muted-foreground">
                        What slipped
                      </p>
                      <ul className="mt-3 space-y-1.5">
                        {s.missed.map((m) => (
                          <li key={m} className="text-sm text-foreground">
                            {m}
                          </li>
                        ))}
                      </ul>
                    </>
                  )}

                  {/* Her Read, which is the half that predicts a score. */}
                  {s.readGiven !== undefined && (
                    <div className="mt-4 border-t border-border pt-3">
                      <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.16em] text-muted-foreground">
                        Your Read
                      </p>
                      <p className="mt-2 text-sm text-foreground">
                        {!s.readGiven
                          ? "You stopped at the retelling. Say what the argument is doing, not only what it says."
                          : s.readMatches
                            ? "You found the move."
                            : "You gave a Read, but not the one this argument turns on."}
                      </p>
                    </div>
                  )}

                  {(s.reciting || s.listing || !s.coloured || (s.coloured && !s.colourSpecific)) && (
                    <div className="mt-4 space-y-1 border-t border-border pt-3">
                      {s.reciting && (
                        <p className="text-sm text-muted-foreground">
                          This one came back close to word for word, so the details on it were
                          carried by the page rather than by you. Say it in your own words instead,
                          even clumsily. That is the version that tells you what you actually have.
                        </p>
                      )}
                      {s.listing && (
                        <p className="text-sm text-muted-foreground">
                          This came out as a run of facts. Tell it as one connected argument.
                        </p>
                      )}
                      {!s.coloured && (
                        <p className="text-sm text-muted-foreground">
                          Nothing of your own on this one. React to it, even briefly.
                        </p>
                      )}
                      {/*
                        * Measured on 18 of 22 in the trial and shown on none.
                        * It is the one standard the whole method turns on: a
                        * reaction that would fit any other stimulus is not a
                        * reaction to this one.
                        */}
                      {s.coloured && !s.colourSpecific && (
                        <p className="text-sm text-muted-foreground">
                          Your reaction would have fitted any stimulus. React to what this one
                          actually claims.
                        </p>
                      )}
                    </div>
                  )}

                  {s.dead_air_seconds >= section.dead_air_threshold_seconds && (
                    <p className="mt-3 text-sm text-muted-foreground">
                      You went quiet for {s.dead_air_seconds}s in the middle of this recall. That is
                      the moment it went, and it is worth finding out what was there.
                    </p>
                  )}

                  {/*
                    * Line by line, because that is how she worked.
                    *
                    * She reads one line silently, looks away and says it back,
                    * and until now the card could only tell her a point went
                    * missing somewhere in the stimulus. The cut is worked out
                    * after the run from the silence in front of each spoken
                    * line, so it is approximate -- which is why it says what she
                    * carried and dropped rather than scoring the line. Coverage
                    * above is unchanged.
                    */}
                  {!!s.lines?.length && (
                    <div className="mt-4 border-t border-border pt-3">
                      <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.16em] text-muted-foreground">
                        Line by line
                      </p>
                      <ol className="mt-3 space-y-3">
                        {s.lines.map((line) => (
                          <li key={line.n} className="rounded-xl bg-[hsl(var(--ac-surface-muted,var(--muted)))] px-3 py-2">
                            <div className="flex items-baseline justify-between gap-3">
                              <p className="text-sm font-medium text-foreground">
                                {line.n}. {line.text}
                              </p>
                              <span className="flex shrink-0 items-baseline gap-3">
                                <span
                                  className={cn(
                                    "text-xs uppercase tracking-[0.12em]",
                                    line.verdict === "strong"
                                      ? "text-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald-strong))]"
                                      : line.verdict === "skipped"
                                        ? "text-[hsl(350_80%_45%)]"
                                        : "text-muted-foreground",
                                  )}
                                >
                                  {line.verdict === "skipped" ? "nothing said" : line.verdict}
                                </span>
                                {hers && line.startMs !== undefined && line.endMs !== undefined && (
                                  <button
                                    type="button"
                                    className="text-xs text-muted-foreground underline decoration-dotted underline-offset-4 hover:text-foreground"
                                    onClick={() =>
                                      void clip.play(`${key}-l${line.n}`, hers, line.startMs, line.endMs)
                                    }
                                  >
                                    {clip.playing === `${key}-l${line.n}` ? "Stop" : "Hear it"}
                                  </button>
                                )}
                              </span>
                            </div>
                            <p className="mt-1 text-sm text-muted-foreground">
                              {line.spoken || "You did not say anything for this line."}
                            </p>
                            {line.note && <p className="mt-1 text-sm text-foreground">{line.note}</p>}
                            {line.dropped.length > 0 && (
                              <p className="mt-1 text-sm text-[hsl(24_85%_42%)]">
                                Dropped: {line.dropped.join("; ")}
                              </p>
                            )}
                          </li>
                        ))}
                      </ol>
                      {s.recall && (
                        <div className="mt-3 rounded-xl border border-border px-3 py-2">
                          <p className="text-sm font-medium text-foreground">
                            The whole thing, text off screen — {s.recall.verdict}
                          </p>
                          {s.recall.note && (
                            <p className="mt-1 text-sm text-muted-foreground">{s.recall.note}</p>
                          )}
                        </div>
                      )}
                      {!!s.bigMisses?.length && (
                        <p className="mt-3 text-sm text-foreground">
                          Biggest misses: {s.bigMisses.join("; ")}
                        </p>
                      )}
                    </div>
                  )}


                  {/*
                    * What the machine heard, and her own voice saying it.
                    *
                    * Both were produced by the grader and thrown away. Reading
                    * the words back is the fastest way to tell a transcription
                    * failure from a memory failure; hearing yourself not say
                    * "only" is the argument ADR 0020 makes for Clips, and the
                    * seek was proved exact on the trial run.
                    */}
                  {(s.spoken || (hers && clipWindow) || model) && (
                    <div className="mt-4 border-t border-border pt-3">
                      <div className="flex flex-wrap items-baseline justify-between gap-x-4 gap-y-1">
                        <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.16em] text-muted-foreground">
                          What you said
                        </p>
                        {/*
                          * Hers, then his.
                          *
                          * The pair is the lesson. One of them is a correction
                          * and two of them is a demonstration, which is the
                          * whole argument for putting a recording on this page
                          * at all.
                          */}
                        <span className="flex gap-4">
                          {hers && clipWindow && (
                            <button
                              type="button"
                              className="text-xs text-muted-foreground underline decoration-dotted underline-offset-4 hover:text-foreground"
                              onClick={() => void clip.play(key, hers, clipWindow.start, clipWindow.end)}
                            >
                              {clip.playing === key ? "Stop" : "Hear it back"}
                            </button>
                          )}
                          {model?.audio && (
                            <button
                              type="button"
                              className="text-xs text-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald-strong))] underline decoration-dotted underline-offset-4"
                              onClick={() => void clip.play(`${key}-model`, model.audio!)}
                            >
                              {clip.playing === `${key}-model` ? "Stop" : "Hear it done right"}
                            </button>
                          )}
                        </span>
                      </div>
                      {s.spoken ? (
                        <p className="mt-2 whitespace-pre-wrap text-sm leading-relaxed text-muted-foreground">
                          {s.spoken}
                        </p>
                      ) : (
                        <p className="mt-2 text-sm text-muted-foreground">
                          Nothing was picked up here.
                        </p>
                      )}
                      {/*
                        * Where the marking came from, said out loud.
                        *
                        * The v1 spec named transcription error as the top risk
                        * and showing her the transcript as the mitigation. The
                        * transcript was shown and nothing said what it was FOR,
                        * so a mis-hearing reads as a verdict about her. In the
                        * audit's run stimulus 5's judge reason quoted her as
                        * saying "clearly moral characters"; the recording says
                        * immoral, and she was told she gave the wrong Read.
                        *
                        * "Hear it back" above is what lets her check, which is
                        * why this sits under the words rather than anywhere
                        * else on the card.
                        */}
                      {s.spoken && (
                        <div className="mt-2">
                          <p className="text-xs text-muted-foreground">
                            The marking is done from these words. If they are not what you said, the
                            marking is wrong, not you. Play it back and say so.
                          </p>
                          <DisputeControl grade={grade} stimulus={s} />
                        </div>
                      )}
                      {model?.walkthrough && <Walkthrough md={model.walkthrough} />}
                    </div>
                  )}
                </div>
              )}
            </li>
          );
        })}
      </ol>

      {onDone && (
        <div className="mt-8 text-center">
          <Button onClick={onDone}>Done</Button>
        </div>
      )}
    </div>
  );
}

/**
 * "This is not what I said."
 *
 * One control, one row, no workflow. It is on the expanded row rather than the
 * card's head because it is about ONE stimulus's verdict, and because the clip
 * and the transcript are right there -- she can check before she claims, which
 * is what makes the claim worth storing.
 *
 * It is drawn as a quiet text button, not a call to action. The card must not
 * invite a student to dispute every band she dislikes; it must make it possible
 * to report the specific failure the audit found, where the app misheard her
 * and marked her down for its own mistake.
 *
 * **It never claims success it did not have.** The table arrives with its own
 * migration, so on a database that has not had it applied the insert fails, and
 * a control that showed "Noted" anyway would be lying on the one screen whose
 * subject is the app getting her wrong. `not-configured` says so plainly.
 */
function DisputeControl({
  grade,
  stimulus,
}: {
  grade: SectionGrade;
  stimulus: SectionStimulusGrade;
}) {
  type State = "idle" | "sending" | "noted" | "unavailable" | "failed";
  const [state, setState] = useState<State>("idle");

  const send = useCallback(async () => {
    setState("sending");
    const result = await raiseDispute({
      assignmentId: grade.assignment_id,
      attemptNo: grade.attempt_no,
      stimulusSlug: stimulus.slug,
      sourceSection: grade.source_section,
      studentId: grade.student_id,
      band: stimulus.reciting ? "recited" : stimulus.band,
      reason: stimulus.readReason || null,
    });
    if (result.ok) {
      setState("noted");
      return;
    }
    // Narrowed by hand rather than by the discriminant: this project runs with
    // `strictNullChecks` off, which weakens union narrowing here.
    const failure = result as { ok: false; reason: "not-configured" | "failed" };
    setState(failure.reason === "not-configured" ? "unavailable" : "failed");
  }, [grade, stimulus]);

  if (state === "noted") {
    return (
      <p className="mt-1.5 text-xs text-muted-foreground" role="status">
        Noted. Joshua will see this beside the run.
      </p>
    );
  }

  if (state === "unavailable" || state === "failed") {
    return (
      <p className="mt-1.5 text-xs text-muted-foreground" role="status">
        That did not save. Tell Joshua directly and he will look at the recording.
      </p>
    );
  }

  return (
    <button
      type="button"
      className="mt-1.5 text-xs underline underline-offset-2 text-muted-foreground hover:text-foreground disabled:opacity-60"
      onClick={send}
      disabled={state === "sending"}
    >
      {state === "sending" ? "Saving..." : "This is not what I said"}
    </button>
  );
}

import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import pt102 from "../content/read-recall-colour-pt102-s3.json";
import pt105 from "../content/read-recall-colour-pt105-s2.json";
import pt134 from "../content/read-recall-colour-pt134-s3.json";
import { classifyDetail, isKindResidue, type DetailKind } from "./grading";

/**
 * Cross-language parity with the Python classifier.
 *
 * `classifyDetail` is a port of
 * `system/scripts/activity-centre/classify_details.py`, and the two have to
 * agree exactly: the Python one produced the census in
 * `corpus/loophole-online/rrc-sections/coverage-baseline.md` that the band
 * floor was measured from, and this one decides what a student is shown. A
 * drift between them is invisible -- scores just move slightly -- so it is
 * asserted against the counts the census was computed with.
 */

type Section = { items: { chunks: { details: { text: string }[] }[] }[] };
const SECTIONS = [pt102, pt105, pt134] as unknown as Section[];

function everyDetail(): string[] {
  return SECTIONS.flatMap((s) => s.items.flatMap((i) => i.chunks.flatMap((c) => c.details.map((d) => d.text))));
}

describe("the classifier agrees with the one that produced the census", () => {
  const details = everyDetail();

  /*
   * 1294 -> 1295 and modality 24 -> 25 on 2026-09-04, when W14 rewrote
   * PT134.S3's key. One detail was added (Q15's conclusion, which the key had
   * recorded as one more fact in a list), and one hedge was marked as a hedge
   * instead of stated as a fact.
   *
   * `content` and `structure` are unchanged, and that is the load-bearing part.
   * The first pass of the rewrite moved six details OUT of structure by naming
   * the speakers -- "This is Tanner's reason." for "This is his reason." -- and
   * this test is what caught it. The vocabulary was widened to take a
   * possessive proper noun rather than the counts being edited to match.
   */
  it("sees the whole authored key", () => {
    expect(details).toHaveLength(1295);
  });

  it("splits it exactly as Python did", () => {
    const counts: Record<DetailKind, number> = { content: 0, structure: 0, modality: 0 };
    for (const text of details) counts[classifyDetail(text)]++;
    expect(counts).toEqual({ content: 1133, structure: 137, modality: 25 });
  });

  it("leaves the same four residue lines, all of them correctly content", () => {
    const residue = details.filter((t) => isKindResidue(t, classifyDetail(t)));

        "This is a rule about companies.",

        "This is how the data were obtained.",

 asking for |
| Nearly there | 72–85% | Holding the argument, losing the detail |
| Thin | 55–71% | The shape survived and the content did not |
| Lost it | below 55% | Read it again |

**Only the top boundary is evidenced, and it is the 10th percentile of the
census** — nine correct runs in ten clear it. It is deliberately not the minimum:
the minimum of any sample is the most fragile statistic available, and here it is
a stimulus whose recording is broken.

**The three lower cutoffs are provisional and are marked as such.** The corpus
contains only correct runs, so it can calibrate where good ends and nothing else.
Where "nearly there" stops being nearly and starts being thin is unknown until
student runs exist, and inventing precision there would be dressing a guess as a
measurement.

**Each kind is judged by its own question.** Details are classified `content`,
`structure` or `modality` at build time, and the judge is asked: for content,
whether she stated the fact; for structure, whether the relationship is present
in how she told it; for modality, whether her qualifier survived. The
classification lives with the build, so a section carries it and the grader
never has to guess.

**The classifier is a closed set plus a residue detector, not a heuristic.**
`structure` and `modality` are matched against authored phrasings that are
enumerated, and anything opening like a discourse role that falls through to
`content` is reported rather than silently accepted. On the three built sections
the closed set leaves 4 residue lines out of 1,294, and all four are correctly
content. A new section's residue is read before it ships; a growing residue
means the authoring vocabulary has drifted, which is worth knowing on its own.

**Numbers above the top band are not ranked.** 100% and 88% are the same
outcome. The Report Card says a run is at the standard and moves on to what she
missed; it does not put her in a race against an unreachable maximum, and it
does not congratulate a difference the measurement cannot support.

## Consequences

**ADR 0012's estimate of the grading job is corrected.** It records "roughly 92
Detail judgements per section". The three built sections carry **426, 419 and
449** Details. 92 is approximately the *chunk* count (80, 82, 87), which is what
the number was when Coverage was still conceived per chunk; ADR 0013 moved
Coverage to the stimulus and the estimate did not follow. The job is about five
times the recorded size. It stays about 25 model calls, because the Details of
one stimulus are judged together.

**Reciting and Listing become load-bearing, not decorative.** With the top band
starting at 86%, a student who recites reaches it trivially. ADR 0012 already
names both failures and puts them in the tips rather than the number. That
division only holds if the tips actually fire, so Reciting is graded in code
against the chunk text and cannot be skipped as a nice-to-have.

**A student can be at the standard and still be told what she lost.** The band
answers "was this run good"; the missed Details answer "what did you drop". They
are different questions and the Report Card shows both, which is what stops the
top band from reading as nothing left to learn.

On 2026-09-08 a grader fix pushed at 02:53 UTC was not running at 03:25 (Cloud tab: "last updated 6 hours ago"; publishing via deploy_project did not change it). Lovable then deployed it on its own at 04:03 UTC as an ai_update "Deployed latest grade-rrc-section", about an hour later and right after another push. So a push does deploy edge functions, but with no visible receipt and no reliable timing; the vault's "auto-deploy on push" line is true only eventually.

**Why:** For an hour the fix looked shipped and was not, while a student waited on a grade. Guessing cost more than checking.

**How to apply:** After any edge function change, prove the deploy from outside: `grade-rrc-section` returns `build` in its 202 body and an `X-Grader-Build` header (bump `BUILD` on every change). Poll for the marker rather than assuming; if it has not changed after ~20 minutes, ask Joshua to nudge Lovable. Related: [[lsatu-content-lives-in-postgres]].

**Fast path (2026-09-08):** a deploy-only message to Lovable's agent via the Lovable MCP `send_message` (project `53b9a6f2-aba7-4ae4-8dd1-fa5b8aab4d76`) works: "Redeploy grade-rrc-section from the repo as it stands, do not edit code, do not publish." It ran `deploy_edge_functions` only, 0.4 credits, and the new build answered within a minute. There is no Supabase access token on the Windows box, so this is the only deploy route.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Safe push to origin/main when Lovable may have committed in parallel.
# Fetches, fast-forwards or merges (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes.

set -e
cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"

# Typecheck gate: tsc does not run during `vite build` (esbuild strips types), so a
# type error (e.g. a missing import that crashes at runtime) would otherwise ship to
# main and auto-deploy. Block the push on any compile error. Set SKIP_TYPECHECK=1 to
# bypass for an emergency push.
if [ "${SKIP_TYPECHECK:-0}" != "1" ]; then
  echo "[safe-push] Typechecking (npm run typecheck)..."
  if ! npm run --silent typecheck; then
    echo "[safe-push] Typecheck FAILED. Fix the errors above or push with SKIP_TYPECHECK=1." >&2
    exit 1
  fi
  echo "[safe-push] Typecheck passed."
fi

git fetch origin main --quiet

LOCAL=$(git rev-parse HEAD)
REMOTE=$(git rev-parse origin/main)
BASE=$(git merge-base HEAD origin/main)

if [ "$LOCAL" = "$REMOTE" ]; then
  echo "[safe-push] Already up to date with origin/main."
elif [ "$REMOTE" = "$BASE" ]; then
  echo "[safe-push] origin/main has no new commits. Pushing."

/**
 * Grade one finished Read, Recall, Colour section.
 *
 * The runner already writes everything this needs and nothing reads it: one
=== stripVerbatimReads
 * Remove each chunk's read-aloud from the words spoken while the text was up.
 *
 * `spokenWorking` arrives as the engine wrote it -- "Thus," and "Hot" and
 * "don't", capitalised and punctuated, because the grader passes Deepgram's
 * `punctuated_word` through. It is normalised here for the comparison ONLY and
 * the cut is made on the original tokens, so what the judge and the student are
 * shown keeps its capitals and its full stops.
 *
 * That normalisation is the second defect this rewrite closes and it was the
 * larger of the two. Before it, the haystack was compared token-for-token
 * against a normalised chunk, so "Thus," could never equal "thus" and only a
 * word that was already lowercase and unpunctuated could match at all. On the
 * twelve captured sections that alone took the reads found from 800 of 956
 * chunks down to 463.
 *
 * Every chunk is located independently over the whole window and the ordering
 * is resolved afterwards, highest-confidence first, admitting a match only if
 * it does not cross one already accepted. A single forward-advancing cursor
 * looks equivalent and is not: one chunk whose read is missing or truncated
 * matches junk somewhere arbitrary, and the cursor then starves every chunk
 * after it. PT134.S3.Q16 is the case that proved it in the corpus -- the
 * recording starts mid-sentence, so chunk 1 matched badly and chunks 2 to 5
 * were all reported unread when every one of them had been read aloud.
 */
export function stripVerbatimReads(spokenWorking: string[], chunks: string[]): StrippedReads {
  // The normalised view of the window, and the original index each word came
  // from, so a span found in one can be cut out of the other.
  const hay: string[] = [];
  const source: number[] = [];
  spokenWorking.forEach((token, i) => {
    for (const w of readWords(token)) {
      hay.push(w);
      source.push(i);
    }
  });

  const candidates = chunks.map((chunk) => {
    const needle = readWords(chunk);
    if (needle.length < MIN_CHUNK_WORDS) return null;
    const best = locateRead(needle, hay);
    return best && best.ratio >= MIN_READ_RATIO ? best : null;
  });

  // Confident matches first: they are the anchors the rest are ordered against.
  const byConfidence = candidates
    .map((span, i) => ({ span, i }))
    .filter((c): c is { span: ReadSpan; i: number } => c.span !== null)
    .sort((a, b) => b.span.ratio - a.span.ratio);

  const accepted = new Map<number, ReadSpan>();
  for (const { span, i } of byConfidence) {
    // He reads the chunks in order, so a read cannot start before a read that
    // is accepted for an earlier chunk, nor after one accepted for a later one.
    let crosses = false;
    for (const [j, other] of accepted) {
      if (j < i && span.start < other.start) crosses = true;
      if (j > i && span.start > other.start) crosses = true;
      if (crosses) break;
    }
    if (!crosses) accepted.set(i, span);
  }

/**
 * Run `worker` over every item, at most `limit` of them in flight.
 *
 * A section is 22 stimuli and each one costs a judge call. Run end to end that
 * is 22 round trips in series, which on the trial run exceeded the edge
 * function's wall clock and returned 504 -- so the student's whole run produced
 * no Report Card, having already been recorded, uploaded and transcribed
 * correctly. Run at once it is one round trip's worth of waiting.
 *
 * Bounded rather than unbounded because the judge is a rate-limited API and a
 * section is not the only thing that might be grading; 429s at the provider
 * would trade one failure mode for another.
 *
 * Results come back in the order of `items`, not the order they finished. The
 * caller writes them into a Report Card the student reads top to bottom, and a
 * section whose stimuli arrive shuffled is worse than a slow one.
 *
 * Nothing here touches the network itself -- the worker does -- so this stays
 * on the pure side of the file.
 */

  items: In[],

const KIND_RULE: Record<DetailKind, string> = {
  content:
    'CONTENT — a fact. Carried if she stated it, in any words, in any order. ' +
    'Synonyms and paraphrase count fully. A dropped proper noun, number or ' +
    'quantifier is NOT carried: "all employees" is missed if she said "the staff".',
  structure:
    'STRUCTURE — a discourse role, not words. Carried if the relationship is ' +
    'present in HOW she told it; she never has to name it. "This is a contrast." ' +
    'is carried by a "but", a "though", or "X is different from Y". "This is the ' +
    'conclusion." is carried by a "so" or "therefore" introducing that claim.',
  modality:
    'MODALITY — the strength of the claim. Carried if her qualifier survived. ' +
    'A flat assertion where the source hedged is NOT carried.',
};

export function buildJudgePrompt(input: {
  stimulus: string;
  entries: JudgeEntry[];
  spoken: string;
  /**
   * The analytical takeaway she should have arrived at, authored on every one
   * of the 1,294 stimuli and, until now, graded by nothing.
   *
   * Coverage answers "did you hold it". The Read answers "did you understand
   * it", and it is the criterion the method is actually built around: the
   * briefing tells her "this is the part that gets checked", the coach rubric
   * says to score it, and the section spec makes it the drill's accuracy
   * criterion. Omit it and the run is judged on memory alone, exactly as it was.
   */
  theRead?: string | null;
  /**
   * One correct run of THIS stimulus, as a document: the verbatim read of each
   * chunk, then the recall of it in his own words.
   *
   * The judge had no example of good work. It carried a checklist of facts and
   * a one-line key for The Read, and was then asked whether her run was a flat
   * list, whether she engaged with the stimulus at all, and whether that
   * engagement was specific to it -- three judgments about quality made against
   * nothing but the model's own idea of quality. This is the standard those
   * three are measured against, and it is the standard a person set.
   *
   * It is emphatically NOT a target to match. A student who reproduced this
   * would be reciting, which is itself a failure the same prompt checks for.
   * The prompt says so at some length, because an exemplar dropped into a
   * grading context without that framing turns into an answer key, and every
   * run that differs from it starts looking wrong.
   */
  modelRun?: string | null;
  /**
   * Her run cut into the lines she was rephrasing, and her whole-stimulus
   * recall on its own.
   *
   * The drill is line by line -- read one line silently, look away, say that
   * line back -- and until now the judge saw the whole thing as one block of
   * text. It could say a fact was dropped; it could not say WHERE, which is the
   * only form of that feedback she can act on. The cut comes from the silence
   * before each spoken line (`rrcLines.ts`), so it is evidence about her run
   * and not a boundary she is scored against.
   */
  lines?: { n: number; text: string; spoken: string }[];
  /** What she said after the text disappeared. Part of `spoken` as well. */
  recall?: string | null;
}): string {
  const kinds = [...new Set(input.entries.map((e) => e.kind))]
    .map((k) => `- ${KIND_RULE[k]}`)
    .join("\n");
  const list = input.entries
    .map((e) => `${e.id}. [${e.kind}] ${e.text}`)
    .join("\n");

    if (aErr) throw aErr;
    if (!assignment) return json({ error: "assignment not found" }, 404);
    if (assignment.mode !== "section") return json({ error: "not a section run" }, 400);

    if (!trusted && assignment.student_id !== uid) {
      const { data: isAdmin } = await admin.rpc("has_role", { _user_id: uid, _role: "admin" });
      if (!isAdmin) return json({ error: "forbidden" }, 403);
    }

    /*
     * Already graded is a success, not a conflict. The client fires this and
     * the sweeper fires it again; the second one must be harmless.
     *
     * Unless the grade on record is a partial one. A section whose judging
     * stage lost some stimuli writes what it has rather than throwing the run
     * away, and that row must not then look finished to the sweeper -- so an
     * incomplete grade is re-driven on sight, without needing `force`.
     */
    const { data: allResponses, error: rErr } = await admin
      .from("activity_item_responses")
      .select("item_order, attempt_no, response_text, rrc")
      .eq("assignment_id", assignmentId)
      .order("item_order");
    if (rErr) throw rErr;
    if (!allResponses?.length) return json({ error: "no responses to grade" }, 409);

    /*
     * The latest Attempt, and only that one.
     *
     * Rows are written as each stimulus finishes, so an abandoned run leaves
     * its stimuli behind and a later sitting adds its own alongside them under
     * a higher `attempt_no`. Grading the lot would score one Report Card across
     * two sittings, with the audio of only the last. The newest Attempt is the
     * run this grade is about.
     */
    const latest = Math.max(...allResponses.map((r) => Number(r.attempt_no ?? 1)));
    const attempt = Number.isFinite(Number(askedFor)) && Number(askedFor) > 0 ? Number(askedFor) : latest;
    const responses = allResponses.filter((r) => Number(r.attempt_no ?? 1) === attempt);
    if (!responses.length) return json({ error: "no responses for that attempt", attempt }, 409);

    /*
     * Already graded is a success, not a conflict. The client fires this and
     * the sweeper fires it again; the second one must be harmless.
     *
     * Checked per ATTEMPT, and therefore only once the attempt is known, which
     * is why it sits below the read rather than above it. Keyed on the
     * assignment alone it answered "has this section ever been graded", so a
     * second sitting was reported as already-graded and never marked -- and if
     * it was marked, it wrote over the first sitting's Report Card. The one
     * this grade is about is the one that has to be absent.
     *
     * Unless the grade on record is a partial one. A section whose judging
     * stage lost some stimuli writes what it has rather than throwing the run
     * away, and that row must not then look finished to the sweeper -- so an
     * incomplete grade is re-driven on sight, without needing `force`.
     */
    const { data: existing } = await admin
      .from("activity_section_grades")
      .select("assignment_id, attempt_no, graded_at, grader_version, section")
      .eq("assignment_id", assignmentId)
      .eq("attempt_no", attempt)
      .maybeSingle();
    const existingIsComplete = (existing?.section as Record<string, unknown> | null)?.complete !== false;
    if (existing && existingIsComplete && !force) {
      const { section: _section, ...summary } = existing;
      return json({ status: "already-graded", ...summary });
    }

    /*
     * Only a whole section is graded.
     *
     * Rows are written as stimuli finish, so a run abandoned at stimulus 3 has
     * rows, and grading them produced a Report Card that read "0%, 2 stimuli,
     * inside the time" and numbered them 1 and 2. The section is the unit of
     * work (ADR 0010); a run that does not cover it is not a run. Refused
     * rather than marked partial, because ADR 0021's partial grade is the judge

describe("the model run as the standard", () => {
  const entries = buildJudgeEntries(toDetails(["All employees are reassigned."]), []);
  const RUN = "> All employees are reassigned.\n\nSo everybody gets moved around.";

  it("carries the correct run when there is one", () => {
    const prompt = buildJudgePrompt({
      stimulus: "A stimulus.",
      entries,
      spoken: "she said something",
      modelRun: RUN,
    });
    expect(prompt).toContain("So everybody gets moved around.");
    expect(prompt).toContain("A CORRECT RUN");
  });

  it("frames it as the standard rather than as the answer", () => {
    // An exemplar dropped into a grading context without this framing becomes an
    // answer key, and every run that differs from it starts looking wrong. Her
    // wording, examples, order and emphasis are all allowed to differ.
    const prompt = buildJudgePrompt({
      stimulus: "A stimulus.",
      entries,
      spoken: "x",
      modelRun: RUN,
    });
    expect(prompt).toContain("It sets the STANDARD, not the answer");
    expect(prompt).toMatch(/Do NOT use it as evidence/);
    expect(prompt).toMatch(/Do NOT reward similarity to it/);
  });

  it("is left out entirely for a section whose runs are not loaded", () => {
    // A section with no walkthroughs grades exactly as it did before, rather
    // than being asked to calibrate against an empty block.
    const prompt = buildJudgePrompt({ stimulus: "A stimulus.", entries, spoken: "x" });
    expect(prompt).not.toContain("A CORRECT RUN");
  });
});

The line verdicts had names and no\n * definitions, so \"partial\" meant whatever the model felt that day; and the\n * card described every run and advised on none of them, so a student at 86%\n * with a lost quantifier learned the number and nothing about the word.\n */\ndescribe(\"next time, and the line scale\", () => {\n  const entries = buildJudgeEntries(toDetails([\"All employees are reassigned.\"]), []);\n  const lines = [{ n: 1, text: \"All employees are reassigned.\", spoken: \"the staff moved\" }];\n\n  it(\"defines every line verdict before asking for one\", () => {\n    const prompt = buildJudgePrompt({ stimulus: \"A stimulus.\", entries, spoken: \"x\", lines });\n    for (const v of [\"strong:\", \"partial:\", \"thin:\", \"skipped:\"]) expect(prompt).toContain(v);\n    expect(prompt).toMatch(/Only a change of MEANING counts against her/);\n  });\n\n  it(\"asks for one concrete thing to do differently, and for nothing when there is nothing\", () => {\n    const prompt = buildJudgePrompt({ stimulus: \"A stimulus.\", entries, spoken: \"x\" });\n    expect(prompt).toContain(\"next_time\");\n    expect(prompt).toMatch(/Leave it EMPTY when there is nothing to fix/);\n  });\n\n  it(\"carries next_time through to the grade, trimmed\", () => {\n    const reply = parseJudgeReply(\n      '{\"verdicts\":[{\"id\":1,\"carried\":false}],\"next_time\":\"  Say the quantifier out loud: all, not some.  \"}',\n      [1],\n    );\n    expect(reply.nextTime).toBe(\"Say the quantifier out loud: all, not some.\");\n    expect(gradeStimulus(entries, reply).nextTime).toBe(\"Say the quantifier out loud: all, not some.\");\n  });\n\n  it(\"treats an absent next_time as nothing to say rather than as an error\", () => {\n    const reply = parseJudgeReply('{\"verdicts\":[{\"id\":1,\"carried\":true}]}', [1]);\n    expect(reply.nextTime).toBe(\"\");\n  });\n});\n"}

describe("hearing it done right", () => {
  const WALKTHROUGH =
    "> The allowable blood alcohol level should be cut in half.\n\n" +
    "So he wants the limit halved, and the reason is social drinkers.";
  const model = () =>
    Promise.resolve({
      PT105_S2_Q1: {
        walkthrough: WALKTHROUGH,
        audio: { bucket: "model-runs", path: "PT105_S2_Q1.mp3" },
      },
    });
  /** The nine authored references: written, and never recorded. */
  const writtenOnly = () =>
    Promise.resolve({ PT105_S2_Q1: { walkthrough: WALKTHROUGH, audio: null } });

  it("offers the model run beside her own recording", async () => {
    render(
      <SectionReportCard
        grade={{
          ...grade,
          section: { ...grade.section, audio_bucket: "drill-recordings", audio_path: "s1/a1.webm" },
          stimuli: [stimulus({ spoken: "x", recall_window_ms: { start: 0, end: 1000 } })],
   * somebody try to learn from one. She cannot skim it, search it, or sit with
   * one line while she works out where her own recall lost a quantifier. The
   * walkthrough is open on the card by default and the recording is the option.
   */
  it("shows the walkthrough without being asked, under her own words", async () => {
    render(
      <SectionReportCard
        grade={{ ...grade, stimuli: [stimulus({ spoken: "x" })] }}
        loadModelRuns={model}
      />,
    );
    fireEvent.click(screen.getByText("Stimulus 1"));
    expect(
      await screen.findByText(/So he wants the limit halved/),
    ).toBeInTheDocument();
    // The verbatim read is rendered as its own line, with the marker stripped.
    expect(
      screen.getByText("The allowable blood alcohol level should be cut in half."),
    ).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("still teaches when the stimulus was never recorded", async () => {
    // Nine of the built stimuli have no recording and never will. Under the old
    // shape the card dropped any run without audio, so those nine could offer
    // her nothing at all.
    render(
      <SectionReportCard
        grade={{ ...grade, stimuli: [stimulus({ spoken: "x" })] }}
        loadModelRuns={writtenOnly}
      />,
    );
    fireEvent.click(screen.getByText("Stimulus 1"));
    expect(await screen.findByText(/So he wants the limit halved/)).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.queryByRole("button", { name: /hear it done right/i })).not.toBeInTheDocument();
  });
});

  it("starts the top band at the census 10th percentile", () => {
    // 86 is measured (ADR 0018) and is not a round number by accident. If this
    // changes, the ADR changed with it or something is wrong.

    expect(bandFor(100)).toBe("at-the-standard");

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu\\docs\\adr\\0035-the-band-floor-belongs-to-the-judge.md", "content": "# 0035 — The band floor belongs to the judge, and is re-measured when the judge moves\n\nDate: 2026-09-08\nStatus: accepted\nAmends [0018](./0018-full-coverage-is-not-a-hundred-percent.md).\n\n## Context\n\nADR 0018 set the top Coverage band at 86%: the 10th percentile of Joshua's own\n71 recordings scored Detail by Detail, so that nine correct runs in ten clear\nit. It then validated the live judge, `gemini-2.5-flash`, against those same\nruns and found it 1.6 points harsher than the hand census with four decoy\nfailures in 71.\n\nOn 2026-09-08 every model call on the platform was moved to `gemini-3.8-flash`.\nThe same student recording, re-graded on the new model, moved from 89 to 86\nmean Coverage; one stimulus fell from 82% to 59%, and six coloured lines became\nnone. Nothing about the recording had changed. The floor had been measured on\none judge and was being applied by another.\n\nThere was no way to re-measure it. The shaped transcripts and the key the\nvalidation used lived on a laptop this machine is not, and the judge is\nreachable only from inside the edge function.\n\n## Decision\n\n**The census runs inside the grader, on demand.** `grade-rrc-section` accepts an\nadmin-only `{ census: { sourceSection, items } }` call: it fetches the model\nruns' own transcripts for that section, cuts the verbatim reads exactly as it\ndoes for a student, judges each stimulus with no walkthrough and no line split\n(the conditions the 0018 census was scored under), and returns one row per\nstimulus without writing anything. `scripts/rrc-census.ts` sends the three\nsections and prints the distribution. Same prompt, same model, same key as a\nstudent's grade, by construction.\n\n**Measured on `gemini-3.8-flash`, build `2026-09-08e`, 71 of 71 stimuli:**\n\n| | Hand census (0018) | 2.5-flash (0018) | 3.8-flash (this ADR) |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Mean | 94.7 | 93.1 | 91.7 |\n| Median | 95 | 94 | 93 |\n| 10th percentile | 86 | 85 | **82** |\n| Minimum | 68 | | 68 |\n| Runs at 100 | 22 | | 20 |\n| Decoy failures | | 4 | **0** |\n| Reads matched | | | 68 of 71 |\n\nThe new judge is about three points harsher than the hand census and about\none and a half harsher than the old judge, and it is cleaner: it never\ncredited a Detail from another stimulus, where the old one did four times.\nThe two runs at 68 are the two ADR 0018 already explained (PT134.S3.Q1's\nfill-in-the-blank conclusion, and PT105.S2.Q25); the recording that starts\nmid-sentence, PT134.S3.Q16, now sits at 82.\n\n**The floor moves to 82.** By 0018's own rule, the 10th percentile of the\ncensus on the judge that is live. The two provisional cutoffs keep their\nwidths below it: nearly there from 68, thin from 51. They are still\nprovisional for the reason 0018 gave.\n\n**The floor is re-measured whenever the judge's model changes**, before any\ngrade written by the new model is shown to a student. `BAND_FLOOR` in\n`rrcGrading.ts` names the ADR that measured it and the model it was measured\non. A floor without a census behind it on the current model is a guess, and\nthe Report Card must not print a guess as a band.\n\n**Two fairness gaps closed in the same prompt.** The line verdicts (strong,\npartial, thin, skipped) had names and no definitions, so the same run could\nland differently on different days; the prompt now defines each one by what\nsurvived. And the prompt says outright that only a change of meaning counts\nagainst her: her words, her order and a plainer phrasing all count in full.\n\n**Every stimulus carries `next_time`.** One or two sentences to her, written\nfrom her run: what to say out loud on purpose, which kind of word she keeps\nlosing, what her Read should have named. Empty when there is nothing to fix,\nand the Report Card then says so rather than going quiet. The grade row also\ncarries the passage and the authored Read, so the card can show the standard\nbeside her words without a query it cannot make.\n\n## Consequences\n\n**Every grade written before 2026-09-08 on 3.8-flash and banded at 86 was\nunder-banded by four points.** Two student runs exist: one graded on\n2.5-flash under the old floor, one on 3.8-flash under it. Both are re-graded\non the live build so they sit on the same judge and the same floor.\n\n**The census costs about 100 seconds and 71 judge calls.** It is run by hand,\nafter a model change, and its rows are saved under `docs/adr/` beside the ADR\nthat cites them.\n\n**0018's other claims stand.** The band, not the percentage, leads; numbers\nabove the floor are not ranked; a recital wears no band.\n"}

function ReportPanel() {

          value={attempt}
          onChange={(v) => setAttempt(v)}
          label="Run"

2054:function makeLabGrade(kind: "clean" | "faults" | "partial"): SectionGrade {
function makeLabGrade(kind: "clean" | "faults" | "partial"): SectionGrade {
  // `over.slug` is the stimulus NUMBER, not the slug. Spread last it would
  // overwrite the slug it was meant to build, and the run history would then
  // look up Details for a stimulus called "7" and find none.
  const stim = ({ slug: n, ...over }: Partial<SectionStimulusGrade>): SectionStimulusGrade => ({
    slug: `PT102_S3_Q${n ?? "1"}`,
    carried: 20,
    total: 21,
    percent: 95,
    band: "at-the-standard",
    missed: [],
    decoysCarried: [],
    unjudged: [],
    reciting: false,
    listing: false,
    coloured: true,
    colourSpecific: true,
    readGiven: true,
    readMatches: true,
    dead_air_seconds: 0.4,
    spoken:
      "so the director says their consultant wants people moved around so everyone is doing what they like and what they are good at",
    recall_window_ms: { start: 12000, end: 48000 },
    ...over,
  });

  const stimuli: SectionStimulusGrade[] =
    kind === "clean"
      ? Array.from({ length: 6 }, (_, i) => stim({ slug: String(i + 1) }))
      : [
          stim({ slug: "1", carried: 20, total: 21, percent: 95, missed: ["This applies to all employees."] }),
          stim({ slug: "2" }),
          stim({
            slug: "7",
            carried: 14,
            total: 19,
            percent: 74,
            band: "nearly-there",
            coloured: false,
            readGiven: false,
            readMatches: false,
            missed: ["The figure is an average.", "This is a contrast."],
          }),
          stim({
            slug: "13",
            carried: 21,
            total: 21,
            percent: 100,
            reciting: true,
            spoken: "the director of ace manufacturing company says our management consultant proposes that we reassign staff",
          }),
          stim({
            slug: "15",
            carried: 12,
            total: 21,
            percent: 57,
            band: "thin",
            dead_air_seconds: 7,
            missed: ["The study ran for four years.", "The nurses were in Sweden."],
            readGiven: true,
            readMatches: false,
          }),
          stim({
            slug: "19",
            carried: 15,
            total: 20,
            percent: 75,
            band: "nearly-there",
            listing: true,
            colourSpecific: false,
            missed: ["This is the reason given."],
          }),
        ];

  const ungraded =
    kind === "partial"
      ? [stim({ slug: "22", carried: 0, total: 18, percent: 0, band: "lost-it", ungraded: "judge timed out", spoken: "" })]
      : [];

  const graded = stimuli.length;
  return {
    assignment_id: "lab",
    attempt_no: kind === "partial" ? 2 : 1,
    student_id: "lab",
    source_section: "PT102.S3",
    transcript_engine: "deepgram/nova-3",
    transcript_text: "",
    grader_version: 1,
    graded_at: new Date().toISOString(),
    stimuli: [...stimuli, ...ungraded],
    section: {
      accommodation: "standard",
      audio_bucket: "drill-recordings",
      audio_path: "lab/lab.webm",
      goal_minutes: 25,
      total_seconds: kind === "clean" ? 1294 : 1294,
                  walkthrough:
                    "> Ace Manufacturing's consultant proposes that we reassign staff so " +
                    "that all employees are doing both what they like to do and what they " +
                    "do well.\n\n" +
                    "So a management consultant is telling us how to run the company, and " +
                    "the idea is to move people around so they are doing the things they " +
                    "like and the things they are good at. Think about McKinsey walking " +
                    "into Amazon and saying, put the people who like driving on the " +
                    "delivery routes.\n\n" +
                    "> This, she says, will \"increase productivity by fully exploiting " +
                    "our available resources.\"\n\n" +
                    "So her claim is that productivity goes up because you are getting the " +
                    "absolute most out of everybody. Well, what if exploit is doing two " +
                    "jobs in this argument? Getting the most out of something, and taking " +
                    "advantage of someone, are not the same word twice.",
                },
              ]),
            )
          }
        />
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

## assistant

Done. Here is where the drill stands.

**One judge, measured fairly.** The grader stays on gemini-3.8-flash. Since the calibration data and key only exist on the Mac, I added an admin-only census route inside the grader itself, so Joshua's 71 model runs are judged by the exact prompt, model and key a student gets. Results on the live judge, recorded in ADR 0035:

| | Hand census (0018) | 2.5-flash | 3.8-flash (live) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Median | 95 | 94 | 93 |
| 10th percentile (top band floor) | 86 | 85 | 82 |
| Decoy failures | | 4 | 0 |

The floor moved from 86 to 82, so the stricter model no longer under-bands anyone. It is also cleaner: it never credited a Detail from the wrong stimulus, where 2.5 did four times. It recognised colour on all 71 of your runs and a Read on 70, so Celia's zero on both is about her run, not the model.

**Clear criteria in the prompt.** The line verdicts strong, partial, thin and skipped now have written definitions. The judge is told outright that only a change of meaning counts against her, never wording or order. Every stimulus now gets a "next time" sentence written from her run, empty when there is nothing to fix.

**The Report Card, per stimulus.** Verified in the browser on the lab fixture:
- The passage, folded, so she can reread it.
- A "Next time" block, or an "Every cylinder" line when every detail, the Read and a reaction of her own were all there.
- Her Read verdict, the judge's reason, and the Read the key turns on.
- Each line: her words, replay, what she held, what she dropped, and your recall of that same line paired directly under hers.
- The whole-stimulus replay, transcript, and dispute control as before.

**Both students re-graded on the same judge and floor.** Celia sits at 87 coverage with 17 of 22 at the standard. Charles sits at 66 with 6 at the standard and 4 lost. Both rows carry advice on all 22 stimuli.

**Not done.** I did not open Celia's real card as her, only the lab fixture. I left the two reply drafts alone as you asked. Five temp scripts under [scripts/](projects/lsatu/scripts) starting with an underscore are mine to delete on your say. Another session's uncommitted drill-hub changes are in the working tree and I did not touch them.

## user

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\session-handoff

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.
